## 2026-08-14 Claude — catalog-wide sweep for the Deckforge Duel defect: a printed number that is not the number applied

After batch 10c, the whole catalog was swept for the fault found in Deckforge Duel — a game that **prints one number and applies another**. All 100 games were scanned three ways. No code changed in this pass; this entry records what was checked and what was found.

- **Pass 1, the exact Deckforge shape: a data table carrying both a number and a description that quotes it, where the code later modifies that field.** Two games matched. One was `deckforge-duel` itself, already fixed. The other was `circuit-draft`, and it is **honest**: `Signal Amp` carries `value: 4` and its text reads "+4 score. +1 for each Logic card.", while the code does `card.value + countTag("logic")` — the text states the modifier exactly.
- **Pass 2, the commoner shape: a claim built in code (`"Deal " + c.damage`) that is separately consumed with a modifier.** One game matched, `forge-freighter`, and it is a **false positive**: the flagged `n + c.value` is a `reduce` accumulator summing each cargo's value, not a modifier applied to a printed one. Each crate contributes exactly the value shown on it, and the separate route bonus is itemised by name on the launch breakdown.
- **Pass 3, the other half of the family: prose that states a rule with a number the code does not have.** This is the fault `bulwark-burst` had, where the header promised a ring that was never drawn. One game matched, `paddle-pulse`, for the "61 degrees" in its help card — and that number is **correct**: it is not a literal in the code because the code computes it from the maximum angle constant, and the batch 10a probe already asserted the help text and the computed value agree.
- **The one thing worth noting, deliberately not treated as a bug.** In `circuit-draft` the small chip on a card face shows `+2` for `Heat Sink`, which scores `2` normally and `4` when heat is already zero. The card's own text says "Cool heat, then score if stable", so the player is told a condition exists. This understates by 2 in one case rather than lying by an unbounded hidden bonus, and the text is not wrong. Recorded as a polish candidate, not a defect.
- **What this sweep can and cannot see.** It is a static scan over the source, so it finds a claim only when the claimed number and the applied number are connected by a named field or by prose. It would not catch a game that prints a number from one source and applies a value computed by a completely separate path with no shared name, nor one whose claim lives only in an image or a cover. Every hit above was read by hand rather than trusted; the conclusion is that **no second instance of the Deckforge Duel defect was found**, not that none can exist.
- Verified: three scanners run over all 100 games with the reports kept, plus by-hand reading of the scoring code in `circuit-draft` and `forge-freighter` and a re-check of the `paddle-pulse` help number against the constant it is derived from.

## 2026-08-14 Claude — overhaul campaign batch 10c: Deckforge Duel printed one number on the card and dealt another

Batch 10 continues (paddle-pulse, bulwark-burst, deckforge-duel, nightwire, slipstream-sprint, lumen-lander). Targeted pass, not a rewrite — the deck, the three enemies, the intent telegraph and the scoring curve are all sound, and the round table and starting deck are byte-identical.

- **Three of the four attack cards printed a damage number they never dealt.** Every card with a damage value silently gains your banked focus *and* a flat `+1` once the combo reaches 3, both of them bare literals inside the play function. Only Strike's text admitted it ("Deal 6 + focus"). Lance said "Deal 10. Pierce 3.", Spark said "Deal 4. Draw 1.", Riposte said "Block 5. Deal 3." — and none of them mentioned focus at all. Measured on the untouched game: **Spark, printed "Deal 4", dealt 5.**
- **Each card now shows what it will actually do right now.** A live line under the printed text reads `NOW 9 damage`, or `NOW 7 past 2 block` when the enemy is guarding, with a gold note saying `spends 3 focus` and `+1 combo` when those apply. The three wrong card texts were corrected to admit the focus bonus. The play function takes its damage from the same helper the card face reads, so the preview and the result cannot disagree.
- **The off-by-one that would have made the preview lie.** The play function increments the combo *before* it works out the bonus, so a preview computed from the current combo would be short by exactly the bonus on the card that first reaches the threshold. The record looks ahead by one. Verified directly: on the card crossing from combo 2 to 3 the preview claimed **7** and the enemy lost exactly **7**.
- **Checked against reality, not just against itself.** A 38-turn scripted duel compared **123 card previews** against a model written separately from the card table parsed out of the file — **zero mismatches** across focus values 0 to 3 and combo values 0 to 6, on both sides of the threshold. Then 15 attacks were actually played: **every one dealt exactly the damage its card had promised**, and all 11 block cards gave exactly the block they promised.
- **The loop never stopped.** A card game sitting still waiting for a click burned **120 frames every 2 seconds** forever. It now runs only while the enemy turn timer, a floating hit number, or a feedback line is live: idle costs **0**, playing a card wakes one chain for 43 frames, and it settles back to **0**. The enemy turn still resolves on real time with the loop gated, verified end to end.
- **Two hazards handled deliberately.** The play path can call the feedback setter mid-frame, which could have armed a rival loop while the frame handle was momentarily zero, so the wake-up is guarded against re-entry. And the loop flags are declared beside the frame stamp at the top, not next to the loop 570 lines below the first function that reads them, because a `let` still in its dead zone would have thrown.
- **Restarting mid-round threw away everything since the last round clear.** Round clears and the end of a run both banked; Restart did not. Measured: a 234-point position restarted to a Best of **234** now, where it used to keep 0.
- **The stored best had no ceiling.** The read rejected `Infinity` and negatives but accepted `1e24`, an unbeatable best forever. Now capped: **eight junk records rejected, four genuine records kept exactly**, including a fractional `12.7` floored to 12.
- **Two layout faults found by looking, not by asserting.** The live line first ran off the card edge into its neighbour, so it now carries only the parts that actually change and leaves the fixed draw and energy text to the card. Then it collided with any description that wraps to two lines, which Spark's does, so the cards are 18px taller and the line hangs off the wrapped text rather than off the card edge. The focus and streak readout also spilled out of the intent panel it sits in, because its width limit was the whole canvas rather than the panel.
- **Checked and already correct, stated as findings not fixes.** The same script gives the same result on a fresh page. The capture recipe's key presses still work. A real five-press Tab walk reaches Restart, Sound and Fullscreen untrapped. Both storage keys are unrenamed. The enemy intent was already telegraphed in full, so nothing here reveals the opponent's plan.
- **Left alone on purpose:** the live line states what a card does, never which card to play, and it says nothing about the enemy that the intent panel did not already say.
- Verified: a 19-assertion Playwright probe covering all of the above, plus desktop and phone screenshots with no sideways scroll at either size. Plus the capture recipe, scoped `test:games`, `capture:games` (desktop and mobile both **0**), `audit:contrast` clean, catalog validation, catalog freeze, `npm test` 56/56, and `git diff --check` clean. Protected meta, JSON-LD, CSP and runtime-script blocks compared against HEAD and identical; the round table and starting deck byte-identical; the card table identical apart from the three corrected text strings, confirmed by comparing it with the text fields masked out.

## 2026-08-14 Claude — overhaul campaign batch 10b: Bulwark Burst told you to watch a ring it never drew

Batch 10 continues (paddle-pulse, bulwark-burst, deckforge-duel, nightwire, slipstream-sprint, lumen-lander). Targeted pass, not a rewrite — the waves, the drone flight, the cannon and the scoring curve are all sound, and the wave table is byte-identical.

- **The header instructed the player to "pulse the shield only when the ring is crowded". There was no ring.** The pulse damages and knocks back every drone within `178` px of the core, and that radius was a bare literal used once inside `pulseShield()` and drawn nowhere. Worse, the decorative background grid draws faint circles at 92, 146 and 200, so the nearest visible circle was not the right one. A dashed ring now sits exactly at the reach, the drones it would catch are individually ringed, and it is labelled with how many are inside. The Shield button reads `Pulse 2` when two are in range.
- **Three numbers on screen, none of them the one that decides.** The cannon refuses to fire above `92` heat, but the Heat pill tinted at 58 and 82 and showed a bare number. The pill now reads `0/92` and tints from the limit that actually refuses the shot. Measured: across 60 attempted shots, 47 were refused and the highest heat that still fired was **90**.
- **Only a win banked your score.** A breach threw the whole run away and so did Restart. Measured before the fix: a run worth **1921** points that ended in a breach left Best at **0**, and a restart at 1515 left it at 0 too. Every way out of a run now commits first, and the win still banks and now names the core bonus it added.
- **The loop never stopped.** `tick()` re-queued unconditionally, so the ready screen and every finished run burned **110 frames every 2 seconds** forever. Measured after gating: the ready screen costs **0**, playing runs **one** chain at 120 frames per 2 s (not two), the world clock advances a real 2 s in 2 s rather than just burning frames, and a finished run winds back down to 0.
- **Two hazards of my own, both handled deliberately.** `update()` calls `updateHud()` and `setFeedback()` mid-frame, and either could have called `ensureLoop()` while the frame handle was momentarily zero and armed a rival loop, so the wake-up is guarded against re-entry. The frame stamp is only reset when a cold chain starts, never inside a per-frame function, so the clock cannot freeze at zero. And rotating the cannon on the sleeping ready screen would have left a stale frame, so that path now repaints once — verified with real arrow-key presses moving the cannon from 270 to 251 degrees with the canvas changing and the mode staying on the ready screen.
- **One rule, one constant.** The reach, the knockback, the cooldown and the heat limit are now named constants that both the simulation and the renderer read, so the drawn ring and the real effect cannot drift apart. A pure `shieldStatus()` feeds the ring, the drone outlines, the button and the diagnostics.
- **Checked against reality, not just against itself.** The in-reach count was compared over **166 consecutive frames** to a count computed separately from the raw drone list using the reach read out of the file — **zero mismatches**, ranging over 0 to 3 inside while up to 5 were on screen. Then a real pulse was fired: **every** drone the ring claimed was hit or knocked back, **every** drone outside was untouched, and the cooldown landed on the stated 4.6 s.
- **The stored best was the weak read again.** `Number(v) || 0` let `Infinity`, `-40`, `12.7` and `1e24` all through, and `Infinity` printed on the Best pill while serializing to null in the diagnostics. Now finite, whole, non-negative and capped at 200000, which is far above the 8620 a measured perfect clear scores: **nine junk records rejected, three genuine records kept exactly**.
- **Checked and already correct, stated as findings not fixes.** The same input script gives the same result on a fresh page. The capture recipe's inputs are all still present. A real five-press Tab walk reaches Pulse, Restart, Sound, Help and Fullscreen untrapped. Both storage keys are unrenamed and are still the only two, and the wave table is byte-identical to HEAD.
- **Left alone on purpose:** the ring is drawn around the player's own core, which is the rule the header already promised, and it says nothing about where drones will be or where to aim. The canvas ring label is small at phone width, but the ring itself and the Shield button both carry the meaning there, so no third copy was added.
- Verified: a 29-assertion Playwright probe covering all of the above, plus desktop and phone screenshots of both the ready screen and mid-run with no sideways scroll at either size. Plus the reduced-motion gate, the capture recipe, scoped `test:games`, `capture:games` (desktop and mobile both **0**), `audit:contrast` clean, catalog validation, catalog freeze, `npm test` 56/56, and `git diff --check` clean. ## 2026-08-14 Claude — overhaul campaign batch 10a: Paddle Pulse hid the one rule that makes it a game of skill

Batch 10 opens here (paddle-pulse, bulwark-burst, deckforge-duel, nightwire, slipstream-sprint, lumen-lander). Targeted pass, not a rewrite — the physics, the AI, the rally and pulse economy and the win condition are all sound.

- **Where the ball meets the paddle is the only thing that sets the return angle, and nothing said so.** The contact offset runs `-1` at the top edge to `+1` at the bottom, and the ball leaves at `offset * MAX_ANGLE` — up to **61 degrees** either way. That single line decided every rally in the game. The paddle was drawn as a plain rounded rectangle with no markings, the HUD read `You, AI, Rally, Pulse, Best`, the status line said only `You returned. Rally 3.`, and the help card said only "Return the ball with the left paddle." The words *aim* and *angle* appeared nowhere on the page. Unlike the last ten games this fact was not even in the diagnostics — it was computed inside `reflectFromPaddle` and thrown away.
- **The rule is now on the paddle, in the HUD, in the status line and in the help.** Five bands are marked on the player paddle in dark ink with the flat-return centre band marked heaviest, a new **Aim** pill reads `33° down` / `Flat` / `30° up`, the status line says `You returned 33° down. Rally 1.`, and the help card states the rule with the same 61 degrees the constant produces. A fading dot-and-ray marks where the ball struck and the line it left on.
- **One rule, one constant.** `reflectFromPaddle` used a local `const maxAngle = Math.PI * 0.34` while the band map would have needed its own copy. Both now read the same `MAX_ANGLE` and `AIM_BANDS`, so the drawn map and the physics cannot drift apart. A pure `aimStatus()` feeds the pill, the status line and the diagnostics.
- **Checked against reality, not just against itself.** Returns were collected across seven different steering biases so contacts landed from offset `-0.48` to `1.00`, and every one was compared to a model written separately from constants read out of the file — **zero mismatches** on degrees, label, pill text and status text. Then the promise was tested against the ball itself: the measured outgoing angle matched the claimed angle to within **0.29 degrees** across every return, a `Flat` return really left with a vertical-to-horizontal ratio of 0.05, and the end-band returns really were steep.
- **The band marks were measurably invisible at first.** Drawn at 0.42 alpha and 1.5px they composited *brighter* than the paddle's own midpoint, so a pixel probe could not tell mark from paddle. Strengthened to 0.62 at 2px, and the check now derives its threshold from the paddle's own measured brightness instead of a number I picked. A mark now sits within 3 pixels of all four band boundaries.
- **A third copy of the angle was tried and dropped.** The degrees were also drawn on the canvas beside the contact point. On both desktop and phone it landed underneath the drag cue and was unreadable, and the pill and status line already carry it. The canvas now shows only the dot and the ray, which is the part the words cannot say.
- **The stored best had no ceiling.** The old read accepted anything finite and positive, so `1e24` survived and printed `1e+24` on the Best pill — a rally no one could ever beat. Now capped at 9999 exchanges, which is far past any real session: **nine junk records rejected, four genuine records kept exactly**, including a fractional `12.7` correctly rounded to 13 and the ceiling value itself.
- **Checked and already correct, stated as findings not fixes.** The best rally is saved the moment it is set, so no reset path can lose it — restarting mid-rally keeps it, verified. The loop was already gated: the ready screen costs **0 frames** and playing runs one chain at 118 frames per 2 s, not two. The fixed-step diagnostic clock lands in exactly the same place whether it is driven in one-frame or two-frame calls, with **zero** drift, and the same script repeats exactly on a fresh page. A real five-press Tab walk reaches Sound, Full Screen and Help with nothing trapping it. Both storage keys are unrenamed and are still the only two.
- **Left alone on purpose:** the aim map is drawn on the player paddle only, not the AI's. Marking the AI's paddle would let a player read the opponent's next angle off the screen, which is answering the shot rather than stating the rule.
- Verified: a 25-assertion Playwright probe covering all of the above, plus desktop and phone screenshots inspected with no sideways scroll at either size. Plus the reduced-motion gate, the capture recipe, scoped `test:games`, `capture:games` (desktop and mobile both **0**), `audit:contrast` clean, catalog validation, catalog freeze, `check-corrupt-storage` across all 100 games, `npm test` 56/56, and `git diff --check` clean. Protected meta, JSON-LD, CSP and runtime-script blocks compared against HEAD and identical, and the two element ids added are both referenced.

## 2026-08-14 Claude — overhaul campaign batch 9f: Chrome Convoy multiplied your whole score by a number it never showed you

Batch 9 closes here (rail-yard-relay, reflex-spark, velvet-heist, signal-siege, cipher-cadence, chrome-convoy). Targeted pass, not a rewrite — the driving, the spawn tables and the stretch progression are all sound.

- **The multiplier ran the entire scoring economy and never reached a pixel.** `state.combo` climbs to `x9` as you gun rivals, and it multiplies *both* halves of the score: every kill is `70 x combo` and every second of driving is `6 x combo`. Any hit knocks it straight back to `x1`. It was published in the diagnostics every frame, and the word "combo" appeared **nowhere on screen** — the HUD read `Stretch, Armor, Heat, Score, Best`. The game printed "Combo lost" for a number the player had never been shown. There is now a **Combo pill** in the HUD and a **combo tag drawn beside the player row** on the canvas, gold from `x2` and green from `x5`.
- **`state.dist` had the same problem.** Progress through the current stretch was computed every frame, published, and never drawn. A **stretch gauge** now stands on the left shoulder: five segments climbing from the bottom, cleared stretches solid, the current one filling as you drive.
- **Every path that clears the multiplier now names what it cost.** `Scraped the edge: the x3 multiplier is gone`. Ramming, blockers, oil, the road edge and both civilian paths all say the number. Measured on a real hit: the status read exactly that while the pill fell to `x1`.
- **A dead increment removed.** Ramming a rival called `killRival` (which bumped the combo) and `damage` (which resets it) in the same frame, so the `+1` was written and wiped before anything could read it. Only the gun builds the multiplier now, which keeps the number on the tag equal to the number that actually scored.
- **Restarting mid-run threw the score away.** Wrecking and winning both banked the best; pressing `R` did not. Measured: a run worth **389** was restarted and the best stayed **0**. It now banks first — same run, best becomes 389.
- **The stored best was unguarded, and my own baseline check missed it.** The read was `Number(v) || 0`, which lets `Infinity`, `-40`, `12.7` and `1e24` all through. The baseline probe had seeded `chrome-convoy.best.v1` — a key this game never reads (the real one is `chrome-convoy:best`) — so every junk record "passed" for the wrong reason. Now finite, whole, non-negative and capped: **nine junk records rejected, three genuine records kept exactly**, and both storage keys are unrenamed.
- **One record, every surface.** A pure `runStatus()` feeds the pill, the canvas tag, the gauge and the diagnostics. Cross-checked over **900 consecutive frames** against a model written separately from constants read out of the file — **zero mismatches**. Then the promise was checked against reality: **every score jump in a full drive was explained by exactly one rule from the file** (gun kill at `70 x combo`, blocker at 40, checkpoint at 120), and the per-second driving rate measured 6/s at `x1` and 18/s at `x3` — exactly three times, as the tag claims.
- **A third caption line on the canvas tag was tried and dropped.** It read as mush at phone width and collided with the number. The pill tooltip and the help card carry the "it multiplies everything" part instead.
- **Checked and already correct, stated as findings not fixes.** The loop was already gated — the menu costs **0 frames** — and driving runs one chain at 120 frames per 2 s, not two. Six seconds of scripted driving gives an identical distance, stretch and score at 60Hz and 120Hz, with **zero** drift. The same input script gives the same state on a fresh page. A real four-press Tab walk reaches Restart, Sound, Fullscreen and Help with nothing trapping it.
- **Left alone on purpose:** the id `touchLayer` is declared and never referenced again, which predates this pass and is cosmetic. Ramming still costs the whole multiplier — that is the balance, and the tag now makes the trade visible rather than silent.
- Verified: a 28-assertion Playwright probe covering all of the above, plus desktop and phone screenshots inspected with no sideways scroll at either size. Plus the reduced-motion gate, scoped `test:games`, `capture:games` (desktop and mobile both **0**), `audit:contrast` clean over 37 measured text elements, catalog validation, catalog freeze, `check-corrupt-storage` across all 100 games, `npm test`, and `git diff --check` clean. - **The timing balance was left byte-identical on purpose.** `TARGET_PROGRESS`, `TIMING_WINDOW`, the two geometry constants and the track table are what the probe's independent judge reads, and changing any of them would alter the game rather than explain it. All confirmed unchanged.

## 2026-08-14 Claude — overhaul campaign batch 9d: Signal Siege wrote every refusal into memory and never put it on screen

Batch 9 continues (rail-yard-relay, reflex-spark, velvet-heist, signal-siege, cipher-cadence, chrome-convoy). Targeted pass, not a rewrite — the waves, the targeting and the scoring are all sound.

- **Every refused tap was explained to nobody.** `buildOrUpgrade()` computed a message for each refusal and then `return`ed *before* `updateHud()`, so the text landed in state and never reached the status line. Measured side by side on the shipped page: after building a Bolt for 55 from 120 credits, tapping the same pad again put `Need 75 credits to upgrade.` into state while the screen still read `Bolt tower online.`; tapping an empty pad with 10 credits left put `Need 55 credits for Bolt.` into state while the screen, again, still read `Bolt tower online.` **A refused tap looked exactly like nothing happening.** One pure `padPlan()` now works out what a tap would do before anything changes, and every refusal renders: `Upgrading Bolt to level 2 costs 75. You have 65.`
- **The numbers that decide the whole game were nowhere on screen.** The three towers differ sharply — Bolt 19 damage at 1.8 shots a second reaching 185, Beam 8 at 5.6 reaching 155, Burst 27 at 1.0 reaching 142 with splash — and the buttons said only `1 Bolt 55`. Each button now carries its damage, fire rate, reach and splash, written straight from the tower table so they cannot drift, with a matching screen-reader name. Picking a different tower also now restates what a tap on the current pad would cost, which it never did.
- **Verified against an independent rule.** A build/upgrade rule and the tower blurbs were written separately from the tower table read out of the file, then compared against the page across **all 27 combinations** of three tower selections and nine pads — verdict, cost, and the exact text on screen. **Zero mismatches.** Then the promise was checked against reality: a build the plan called affordable cost **exactly** the stated price, and a refused upgrade spent **nothing**.

There are only six waves, so it is now finite, positive, whole and capped at six: **twelve junk records rejected, three genuine records kept exactly**.

- **The earlier deliberate fix was preserved and proved.** A previous pass added a `waveKills > 0` gate so a player who builds nothing and lets a whole wave leak cannot bank the clear bonus, with a comment explaining why. That is untouched and still holds: a wave leaked with no towers ends `Wave leaked through -- no bonus`, score **0**, best **0**, credits unchanged at 120, core down to 84.

Driving the real loop from a synthetic clock at 60Hz and at 120Hz for two seconds moved the enemies to within **one pixel** of each other, so there is **no double-speed defect** — that single pixel is clock-seeding rounding, not a doubling. ## 2026-08-14 Claude — overhaul campaign batch 9c: Velvet Heist let one stray step quietly break the whole route

Batch 9 continues (rail-yard-relay, reflex-spark, velvet-heist, signal-siege, cipher-cadence, chrome-convoy). Targeted pass, not a rewrite — the sightlines, the routes and the scoring are all sound.

- **One step off the planned beat permanently offset everything that followed, and the game kept giving advice that could not work.** An off-route move into an unwatched cell left the thief standing on the new square while `routeIndex` stayed put, so every remaining planned step was measured from the wrong place. Measured on the shipped page: from the start square, one step north, then following the game's **own** recommendation three times ended in `No case here to lift` — a failure worded as the player's mistake, when the case was simply one row away because the whole route had shifted. The thief now **holds position** when a beat is broken: the alarm still rises, the route stays intact, and the message says exactly that — `Off the planned beat, so the thief held position to keep the route intact. 1 more raises the alarm.`

That behaviour is unchanged: the probe found a moment where an off-plan neighbour is watched, took it, and the run still ended with `Caught in a museum sightline`. Only the step into a **dark** cell pulls back.
- **Restart threw away a score the pill was already calling the Best.** `restart()` zeroed the score without banking it. Measured: a run worth **110** with nothing in storage, Restart pressed, and the best reverted to the old value with `localStorage` still empty — while the pill had been showing 110 as Best the whole time. One `commitBest()` on the restart path fixes it, and the same run now stores 110.
- **Storage.** `Number(x) || 0` let a fractional `12.7` through as a best, and `Infinity` slipped into the `Math.max` the HUD and diagnostics both read, where it serialized to `null` while still counting as unbeatable. Now finite, positive, whole and capped: **twelve junk records rejected, three genuine records kept exactly**.
- **Every message was painted success-green,** including `Caught in a museum sightline` and the alarm warnings. The status line is now toned by what happened.
- **Verified against an independent model.** A route tracer and sightline model were written separately from the `STAGES` table read out of the file, and compared against the page across **all 66 planned beats of all four rooms** — thief position, recommendation wording and the exact lit-cell set at every beat. **Zero mismatches**, and following the plan end to end still **wins the heist** for 894 points.
- **Checked and already correct, stated as findings not fixes.** The menu settles to **zero** animation frames once its opening cue expires. Scripted play repeats exactly across fresh pages. Tab reaches the touch pad with nothing trapping focus. The capture recipe still follows the route and lifts a case. And the planned route never walks the thief into a light — the plan's own steps are judged against the sightlines *after* the guards move, and all 66 of them are safe, so there is no stale-sightline trap on the route itself.
- **A probe bug worth recording rather than a game bug.** An off-route step is judged by the sightlines **currently drawn**, while a planned step is judged after the guards advance. My first model used the post-step lighting for both and wrongly reported the sightline catch as broken. The two checks genuinely differ, and the off-route one matches exactly what the board shows.

Batch 8 complete (breachline, shadow-switch, shadow-vault, volt-sudoku, checkers, chess). Targeted pass, not a rewrite — this engine is in genuinely good shape.

- **The endings were chess jargon with no explanation, and the code proves one was intended.** The Game Over card showed only a title: `Draw — Insufficient material`, `Draw — Fifty-move rule`, `Draw — Threefold repetition`, `Draw — Stalemate`. A casual player has no way to know what any of those mean or why the game just stopped. And the source contained **three dead `const sub = 'Result: …'` assignments** — computed at each checkmate and stalemate branch and **never read by anything**, because the card had no sub-line element at all. The intended second line was written and dropped. Each ending now explains itself in plain words and finally shows the result score: `Neither side has enough pieces left to force a checkmate. Only the two kings are left. Result: 1/2-1/2.`, `100 half-moves passed with no capture and no pawn move, so neither side is making progress. Result: 1/2-1/2.`, `The same position came up three times with the same side to move… Result: 1/2-1/2.`, `White has no legal move but is not in check, so nobody wins. Result: 1/2-1/2.`, `Black's king is under attack and has no legal move left. Result: 1-0.`
- **One record drives every surface.** A pure `describeEnding()` feeds the overlay title, the new overlay sub-line, the screen-reader announcement and the diagnostics, so they cannot describe the ending differently. The insufficient-material case also names what is actually left on the board. - **Cross-checked against an independent judge.** The insufficient-material and fifty-move rules were compared against a separately written implementation across **12 positions with zero mismatches** — K vs K, K+B vs K, K+N vs K, same-colour bishops (draws), opposite-colour bishops, two knights, K+P, K+R, K+Q, the start position (not draws), plus 99 and 100 half-moves either side of the clock.
- **Two probe bugs of mine, both the same lesson as checkers.** `loadFEN()` is **pure** — it returns a state rather than installing one — so my first attempt tested positions that were never loaded and quietly played moves on the standard opening. And `applyMove()` **flips the turn itself**, so my extra flip handed the check back to the side that had just moved and no checkmate or stalemate could ever fire. Both fixed before any conclusion was drawn.
- **Checked and already correct, stated as findings not fixes.** Three seconds untouched costs **zero** animation frames. All five terminals fire correctly and the draw gate passed **before and after**. Castling works on both wings and lands the rook on f1 and d1; en passant removes the passed pawn; promotion produces the chosen piece; selecting a pawn highlights exactly its real targets and nothing more. Undo takes back a checkmate and clears the recorded ending, a new game clears it too, and closing the card now clears the reason text so a stale explanation can never flash on a later ending. Twelve junk storage values leave a valid, playable game, and the single storage key is untouched.
- **Found and deliberately left alone.** The promotion picker's `promoChoices` id is referenced by nothing — pre-existing dead markup I did not add. Removing an id is a cosmetic change with a small blast radius and no benefit, so it is recorded rather than touched. Separately, `goDetail` was at first only reachable through the browser's implicit id-to-global binding; that was replaced with an explicit lookup rather than left to work by accident.
- **The draw-rules block was left byte-identical on purpose.** The gate extracts the delimited `>>> chess-draw-rules` block and evaluates it standalone in Node, and greps `postMoveChecks`'s body with a regex that stops at the first closing brace in column one. Confirmed unchanged, still evaluating standalone and still exporting all four functions, with the body still captured and all three draw calls intact.
- Verified: a 34-assertion Playwright probe covering all of the above, plus scripted play repeating exactly across fresh pages, the capture recipe's opening move still working, Tab moving focus off the board with nothing trapping it, and a phone check with the whole reason unclipped and no sideways scroll. The same helper also skipped `pushHistory()`, which made a later undo test look like the game had failed to restore a captured piece. Both fixed, and every ending assertion re-run against real moves.

Undo mid-game restores the board exactly, and a finished game correctly **refuses** undo because the board is locked behind the modal, so a recorded ending can never be orphaned. Ten junk storage values leave a valid, playable game, and the single storage key is untouched.
- **Insufficient material: checked, no separate rule needed.** Termination is already guaranteed — the no-progress clock ends any dead position within 80 plies, and in the canonical king-versus-king case threefold fires far sooner, measured. Adding a dedicated dead-position rule would duplicate a terminal that already fires.

## 2026-08-14 Claude — overhaul campaign batch 8c: Shadow Vault drew every cone one turn behind the check that catches you

Batch 8 continues (breachline, shadow-switch, shadow-vault, volt-sudoku, checkers, chess). Targeted pass, not a rewrite — all 4 vaults are solvable and the turn order is already correct.

- **The cones you planned against were never the cones that judged you — on every single turn.** A turn is: you step, the guards step, *then* your arrival cell is checked. The board drew each guard's vision at its **current** square, so the amber light on screen was always one turn out of date. Every guard on levels 1-4 has speed ≥ 1, which in this rule means it moves **every** turn, so the mismatch was not occasional — it was total. Proved with the solvability gate's own independent model: a search found a "looks dark, actually lit" step on **all four** levels, and on Cipher Hall it is available on the **very first move**. Then confirmed on the shipped page: standing at `(4,4)` with the guard drawn at `(7,4)` facing east and its cone showing only `(8,4)`, stepping right to `(5,4)` — outside every cone drawn on screen — took alert from **0 to 28**.
- **What the board shows now.** The incoming light (the guards' positions and cones *after* they step) is drawn as red dashed outlines, the current light is kept faint so you can still read the sweep direction, each of the four steps carries a mark in its tile corner — green clear, amber blocked, red with a cross for spotted or lockdown — and a standing line reads the queued move out: `SPOTTED BY GUARD 1 — That cell is dark now, but guard 1's light sweeps onto it when the guards step. Alert 0 to 28, 3 spots from lockdown.`
- **It cannot disagree with the game.** One pure `outlookFor()` replays the whole turn on throwaway copies — door test, block test, key pickup, the guards' step, the spotting check and the exit credit, in the order `tryMove()` runs them — built on the same `blockedIn()`, `visibleCellsIn()` and `stepGuards()` primitives the live turn uses (`tickTurn()` now literally calls `stepGuards`). Verified two ways: **200 published verdicts compared against a separately written model of the rules with zero mismatches**, and **40 executed moves each checked against its own recorded prediction** — every one landed on the promised square with the promised alert.
- **The alert rule was nowhere on screen.** Being spotted costs 28, a clear turn refunds 3, bumping steel costs 5, and lockdown is 100 — none of it stated. The verdict line now names the alert before and after and how many spots remain, the spotted message says the same, and the blocked message admits its `Alert +5`. The pill's danger colour is now **derived from the rule** rather than a round number: it turned red at 70, but 70 + 28 = 98 survives, so the true edge is **72**.
- **Storage.** `Number(x) || 0` let `Infinity` through (printed `Infinity` on the pill *and serialized to `null`* inside `render_game_to_text()`, breaking the diagnostics contract while staying valid JSON), plus `-40`, `1e24` as `1e+24`, and a fractional `12.7`. Now finite, positive, whole and capped: **14 junk records rejected, 4 genuine records kept exactly**.
- **Found, deliberately NOT changed, and my first reason for that was wrong.** A guard's cone passes **through solid walls**: at `(8,4)` facing west it sees `(7,4)`, skips the wall column at `x=6`, and resumes at `(5,3) (5,4) (5,5)` — because the `break` leaves only the offset loop and never the range loop. My first justification for leaving it was that repairing it would destroy the "step onto a lit exit" witness the solvability gate demands. **I tested that and it is false** — the witness survives unchanged on every level. The real reason is difficulty: blocking cones at walls removes **82% of level 1's cone cells (11 to 2)**, 50% of level 2 and 46% of level 3, which would gut the stealth of four authored levels I cannot rebalance without editing the frozen level data the gate parses by shape. The leaked cells are drawn exactly as they are enforced, so this is odd rather than hidden. **Open finding, carried forward.**
- **Checked and already correct, stated as findings not fixes.** Four seconds untouched costs **zero** animation frames and moves nothing — the loop is already gated on the spotted pulse. It is turn-based with no real-time simulation, so there is **no double-speed defect**. And best is banked on **every** stage clear, verified by actually solving Service Duct with a 21-move winning path: **352 earned, 352 stored, 352 after a restart, 352 after a reload** — this game does **not** have the restart-discards-score defect.
- **The levels literal was left byte-identical on purpose.** The solvability gate parses `const levels = [ ... ]` by shape, so reformatting it would break the gate rather than the game. Confirmed unchanged and still parseable, and the gate ran **before and after**: all 4 vaults still winnable with a reachable lit-exit arrival.
- Verified: a 34-assertion Playwright probe covering all of the above, plus a run following only the board's own verdicts finishing 160 moves with the alert never leaving **0**, reduced motion reporting the live state under both `reduce` and `no-preference` with no page errors, the capture recipe still starting the route, scripted play repeating exactly across fresh loads, Tab moving focus off the board with nothing trapping it, and a phone check with the pad on screen, the verdict unclipped and no sideways scroll. Plus `check-shadow-vault-solvable` and `check-reduced-motion` before and after, scoped `test:games`, `capture:games` (desktop and mobile both 0), `audit:contrast` clean, catalog validation, catalog freeze, `check-corrupt-storage` across all 100 games, `npm test` 56/56, and `git diff --check` clean. Protected meta, JSON-LD and CSP blocks compared byte for byte against HEAD and identical, the runtime script tag unchanged, both storage keys untouched, and no unreferenced element ids.

## 2026-08-14 Claude — overhaul campaign batch 8b: Shadow Switch drew the cones one step behind the check that catches you

Batch 8 continues (breachline, shadow-switch, shadow-vault, volt-sudoku, checkers, chess). Targeted pass, not a rewrite — all 4 floors are solvable and the turn order is already correct.

- **The cones you planned against were not the cones that judged you.** A turn is: you arrive, you are checked, the guards step, you are checked *again*. Only that first check used the amber cones drawn on the board, so the light that swept in on the second check was **invisible**. A cell could be clear of every cone on screen and still end the floor. Proved, not argued: the probe walked the floor over safe steps and at depth **14** found a step to `(6,5)` that was **outside every cone drawn on screen**, executed it, and the floor ended in a capture. The board now outlines the incoming light in dashed red, and marks each of the four steps you could take with a coloured dot — green safe, amber clear, red with a cross for caught — plus a standing verdict line under the board: `SAFE STEP — No cone reaches that cell after the guards step.` or `CAUGHT BY G1 — That cell is dark now, but G1's light sweeps onto it when the guards step.`
- **It cannot disagree with the game.** All of it comes from one pure `turnOutlook()`. Verified by sweeping every step at every node of a sixteen-step walk, collecting each distinct verdict, then **replaying each on a fresh page**: a blocked step moved nobody and did not advance the counter, a caught step really ended the floor with the matching event, and a safe step moved you and left the floor live. A run that follows only the board's own verdicts is never caught.
- **The danger mark could be hidden, then it could not be read.** First version drew the dots inside `drawSight()`, under the objects layer, so a key or a switch sitting on a step covered its mark; and at 24-30% alpha they read as grey on the dark board. Both found by **looking at the rendered screenshot**, not by a passing test. The marks now draw last, in the tile's top-right corner, at full strength.
- **Storage: the saved best accepted numbers that are not scores.** The guard was `typeof x === "number"`, which is true for `Infinity` and for negatives. Measured: `{"steps":-5,"time":-5}` loaded a best of `-5` that **no real run can ever beat**, so the scoreboard would be stuck on it forever; `1e999` printed `Infinity/Infinitys` on the pill *and serialized to `null` inside `render_game_to_text()`*, breaking the diagnostics contract while still being valid JSON; `1e24` printed `1e+24`. Now finite, not negative, whole steps, and both figures capped.
- **A near-miss worth recording.** The first version of that guard declared its two ceiling constants *below* the state object that calls `loadBest()`, so every read threw inside the `try` and silently returned `null` — junk **and** genuine records were both discarded. Every junk assertion still passed, for entirely the wrong reason. It was caught only because the same test also asserted that a **valid** record survives. A storage-guard test that only feeds it garbage cannot tell a working guard from a broken one.
- **Checked and already correct, stated as findings not fixes.** Best records a **full four-floor run**, so a caught or restarted partial run has nothing to bank — this game does **not** have the restart-discards-score defect. It is turn-based with no real-time simulation, so there is **no double-speed defect**, and eleven seconds untouched moves neither a guard nor the player.
- **The levels literal was left byte-identical on purpose.** The solvability gate parses `const LEVELS = [ ... ]` out of the page by shape, so reformatting it would break the gate rather than the game. Confirmed unchanged and still parseable, and the gate ran **before and after**: all 4 floors still winnable, and a reachable lit-exit arrival is still a capture rather than a win.
- Verified: a 25-assertion Playwright probe covering all of the above, plus reduced-motion reporting the live state under both `reduce` and `no-preference` with no page errors, twenty corrupt saved records leaving a sensible best, eleven valid-and-invalid best records classified correctly, scripted play repeating exactly across fresh loads, Tab moving focus off the board with nothing trapping it, and a phone check with the pad on screen, the verdict unclipped and no sideways scroll. Plus `check-shadow-switch-solvable`, `check-reduced-motion` and `check-playwright-harness` before and after, scoped `test:games`, `capture:games` (desktop and mobile both 0), `audit:contrast` clean, catalog validation, catalog freeze, `check-corrupt-storage` across all 100 games, `npm test` 56/56, and `git diff --check` clean. Protected meta, JSON-LD and CSP blocks compared byte for byte against HEAD and identical, the runtime script tag unchanged, both storage keys untouched, and no unreferenced element ids.

## 2026-08-14 Claude — overhaul campaign batch 8a: Breachline drew the patrol cones one beat behind the check that catches you

First landing of batch 8, which is the six games that ship their own dedicated correctness gates: breachline, shadow-switch, shadow-vault, volt-sudoku, checkers and chess. A targeted pass on this one, not a rewrite — the missions are all solvable and the loop is already correct.

- **The cones you planned against were not the cones that judged you.** Patrols step at the same instant your agents do, and the sight check runs *after* that step — but the board drew each patrol's vision at the **current** beat. So a square could look completely clear while you were planning and still be inside a cone when the beat resolved, ending the run instantly with no warning. Proved, not argued: the probe walked the mission over safe beats and at depth six found a move whose destination was **outside every cone drawn on screen**, executed it, and the run **failed**. The board now paints the next beat's guard squares and cones in red, keeps the current ones faint so you can read the sweep direction, and the danger set it warns about is confirmed different from the currently-drawn one.
- **Four different failures shared one message.** A beat could be refused because an agent would step off the grid, walk into a wall, walk into a patrol, because both agents targeted the same non-exit square, or because they tried to swap places — and every one of them said only `Route blocked. Re-queue the beat.` Each now names itself, and a standing verdict line under the board reads the queued beat out before you commit: `SAFE BEAT — Neither agent ends inside a cone once the patrols step. +8`, `SPOTTED BY P1 — Beta ends the beat inside P1's cone once the patrols step. That ends the run.`, or `BEAT BLOCKED — Alpha would walk into a wall. Nothing moves.`
- **It cannot be wrong.** All of it comes from one pure `beatOutlook()` that replays the entire beat on throwaway copies — both moves, all four block tests, the core pickup, the patrol step and the sight check — and `executeBeat()` reports from the same verdict. Verified by sweeping every one of the 25 command pairs at every node of a fourteen-beat walk, collecting each distinct verdict, then **replaying each on a fresh page**: a blocked beat moved nobody and did not advance the counter, a spotted beat really ended the run with the matching event, and a safe beat advanced exactly one beat with nobody caught.
- **Restart threw the run away.** Best was written only when a mission cleared, so a run that scored and was then spotted or restarted banked nothing — measured at 48 points earned and nothing stored. One `bankBest()` now runs on a mission clear, on being spotted, and before either restart path resets the score.
- **Storage.** `parseInt` already stopped `NaN` and `Infinity`, but `-5` came through as a negative best and a huge value printed on the scoreboard as `1e+24`. Now positive, whole and capped.
- **Checked and already correct, stated as findings not fixes.** The loop is gated on a decorative pulse and sleeps otherwise — an untouched board costs **zero** animation frames and eleven seconds untouched changes nothing. This is a turn-based game with no real-time simulation, so there is **no double-speed defect** to report.
- **The missions literal was left byte-identical on purpose.** The solvability gate parses `const missions = [ ... ]` out of the page by shape, so reformatting it would break the gate rather than the game. Confirmed unchanged and still parseable, and the gate was run **before and after**: every mission still has a reachable win.
- Verified: a 22-assertion Playwright probe covering all of the above, plus a run that follows only the board's own verdicts never getting spotted, twenty corrupt saved scores leaving a finite whole best with zero page errors, scripted play repeating exactly across fresh loads, Tab moving focus off the board with nothing trapping it, the touch pad exposing a button for every command, and a phone check with the Execute button above the fold, the verdict unclipped and no sideways scroll. Plus `check-breachline-solvable` before and after, scoped `test:games`, `capture:games` (desktop and mobile both 0), `audit:contrast` clean, catalog validation, catalog freeze, `check-corrupt-storage` across all 100 games, `npm test` 56/56, and `git diff --check` clean.
