## 2026-08-14 Claude — overhaul campaign batch 7f: Orbit Salvage's only aiming aid was a straight line in a gravity game

Sixth landing, and **batch 7 is complete**. A targeted pass, not a rewrite. The physics, the levels and the loop were all sound, so the work went into the one thing a slingshot puzzle owes the player: some idea of where the shot actually goes.

- **The aim indicator pointed where gravity was not.** The only aiming aid was a short dashed line from the station in the launch direction — which is where the skiff would travel with **no gravity at all**, and therefore never where it goes. Every launch was blind guess-and-check, and each miss costs 10 score outright plus 35 off the sector bonus. The board now draws the arc the shot **really** flies, fading out over about 1.6 seconds, in amber normally and red when that arc ends in a gravity well.
- **It cannot drift from the real flight, structurally.** One frame of flight physics was extracted into a pure `flightStep()` on a plain body, and the live flight and the preview now step through **the same function**, so the drawn arc and the flown path are the same computation. Proved rather than asserted: across **six genuinely different launches** the probe compared the arc drawn before launch against the position the skiff actually occupied on every one of ~98 frames, and the largest gap anywhere was **under a hundredth of a pixel**. The same arcs were also checked against an independent integrator written from the posted rules, and a shot the arc paints red was launched and did collide with the reported `Gravity well collision.`
- **Deliberately partial, and I want that on the record.** The preview covers only the first hundred frames of a flight that can run sixteen seconds. That is enough to teach how each planet bends the shot without simply handing over the solution. Showing the whole path, or naming which pods a shot would collect, would have solved the puzzle for the player, so I did not.
- **The shots pill showed a different number from the one being charged.** The sector bonus deducts 35 for every shot after the first **in the current sector**, but the pill displayed the whole run's shot count, so from sector two onward the number on screen was not the number the penalty used. It now leads with the sector count, keeps the run total in brackets when they differ, and the payout formula is one shared function whose result is published, so the readout and the award cannot drift. Verified: three launches dropped the published payout 560, 525, 490.
- **Storage.** `Number(readStore(BEST_KEY,"0")) || 0` let `Infinity` and `1e999` through, printing `Infinity` on the scoreboard and, since `JSON.stringify` writes Infinity as `null`, breaking the diagnostics contract. `-5` printed a negative best and `1e24` printed as `1e+24`. Now finite, positive, whole and capped.
- **Three things checked and found already correct, stated as findings not fixes.** The loop already consumes real elapsed time with the per-frame gap clamped and already sleeps — an untouched board costs **zero** animation frames, eleven seconds untouched changes nothing, and 60Hz and 120Hz runs advance the flight clock identically across twice the frames, so **there is no double-speed defect here**. Restarting does **not** discard the score: it is banked on every crash and every dock. My first probe reported it missing, and an isolated re-check showed 110 earned, 110 stored, 110 still there after restart — the probe was wrong, not the game.
- **The levels literal was left byte-identical on purpose.** The solvability gate parses `const levels = [ ... ]` out of the page with a shape-sensitive pattern, so reformatting it would have broken the gate rather than the game. Confirmed unchanged and still parseable, and the gate was run **before and after** the change: every sector still has all pods rescuable and its beacon dockable.
- Verified: a 27-assertion Playwright probe covering all of the above, plus twenty corrupt saved scores leaving a finite whole best with zero page errors, a crashed run banking its score through a reload, the real capture recipe still launching the skiff past the first gravity well, `advanceTime` byte-identical across two fresh loads, Tab moving focus off the board with nothing trapping it, `reducedMotion` reporting correctly under both reduce and no-preference with no console errors, and a phone check at the opening and after eight launches with the launch button above the fold, the shots pill unclipped and no sideways scroll. Plus, run in full, **`check-orbit-salvage-solvable`** and the **`check-reduced-motion` gate (35 games)**, both of which list this game, scoped `test:games`, `capture:games` (desktop and mobile both 0), `audit:contrast` clean, catalog validation, catalog freeze, `check-corrupt-storage` across all 100 games, `npm test` 56/56, and `git diff --check` clean. The corrupt-storage sweep hit the known `ERR_NO_BUFFER_SPACE` transport error once on an untouched game; a clean re-run passed all 100. Protected meta and JSON-LD blocks compared byte for byte against HEAD and identical, the runtime script tag unchanged, and both storage keys left untouched.

## 2026-08-14 Claude — overhaul campaign batch 7e: Skyline Sentry's shield pulse failed silently three ways and wasted itself a fourth

Fifth landing of batch 7. A targeted pass, not a rewrite. This is an action game so the loop was audited first and came back clean, which is stated plainly below rather than dressed up as a fix.

- **The only defensive ability had four outcomes and told you about none of them.** Pressing Pulse did nothing at all, with no message and no sound, when the run was not live, when it was still recharging, or when the shield was under the 28 it costs. Worse, pressing it with **no drone inside its range spent 28 shield and 3.2 seconds of cooldown, hit nothing, and reported success**: `Shield pulse armed the skyline barrier.` Measured on the old build with an empty sky — shield 100 down to 72, cooldown 3.2, zero drones touched. Every one of those is now a spoken refusal that costs nothing, and the wasteful one is refused outright: `No drone is inside pulse range yet, so a pulse would spend 28 shield for nothing. Let them close in.`
- **The button never changed, ever.** It read `Pulse Shield` whether it was ready, recharging, short of shield, or aimed at empty sky — the code that keeps the rest of the panel in step simply never touched it. It now reads `Pulse 3 in range`, `Pulse 2.4s`, `Pulse needs 28` or `Pulse 0 in range`, all from one pure `pulseState()` that the label, the refusal message and the pulse itself run from, so the three cannot disagree. Verified by reaching all four states in a real run and checking the label, the refusal, the shield and the cooldown at each.
- **The shield was doing the work and getting no credit.** A drone reaching the core absorbs `18 + 6 per strength` into the shield and leaks the rest into the core, so the shield is what decides a long run — and the old line said only `Core impact on middle lane. Integrity 92.` It now reads `Shield absorbed 24, core took 8, integrity 92.` Checked against an independent reading of the posted rule, and against what the integrity actually did.
- **Storage.** `Number(readStore(BEST_KEY,"0")) || 0` let `Infinity` and `1e999` through, printing `Infinity` on the scoreboard and, since `JSON.stringify` writes Infinity as `null`, breaking the diagnostics contract. `-5` printed a negative best and `1e24` printed as `1e+24`. Now finite, positive, whole and capped.
- **No timing bug, and I checked rather than assumed.** The loop already consumes real elapsed time with the per-frame gap clamped, and it already sleeps: an untouched board costs **zero** animation frames and eleven seconds before Start changes nothing. Driving 60Hz and 120Hz runs from the same synthetic clock base gave the **same wave time, the same spawn count and the same drone positions** across twice as many frames. **This game does not have the double-speed defect.**
- **No score loss on restart either.** Best is written continuously while playing, so restarting mid-run keeps it — measured, not assumed, and worth saying because six of the last ten games did lose it. My first probe reported the score missing from storage, which turned out to be the probe reading before the run had banked; a focused re-check showed 133 earned, 133 stored, 133 still there after restart.
- **No rebalance.** A player who simply aims at the closest drone and pulses when two or more are in range **clears all five waves for 2966**, so the game is winnable and the difficulty is fair. Nothing changed.
- Verified: a 30-assertion Playwright probe covering all of the above, plus a losing run banking its score through a reload, twenty corrupt saved scores leaving a finite whole best with zero page errors, the real capture recipe still starting the sentry and firing into a lane with the pulse verdict in the frame, `advanceTime` byte-identical across two fresh loads, Tab moving focus off the board with nothing trapping it, and a phone check at both the opening and a **deep wave** with the lane buttons above the fold, the pulse label unclipped and no sideways scroll. The reduced-motion requirement was checked directly too: `reducedMotion` reports true under reduce and false under no-preference, both with no console or page errors. Plus, run in full, the **`check-reduced-motion` gate (35 games)** which lists this game, scoped `test:games`, `capture:games` (desktop and mobile both 0), `audit:contrast` clean, catalog validation, catalog freeze, `check-corrupt-storage` across all 100 games, `npm test` 56/56, and `git diff --check` clean. Protected meta and JSON-LD blocks compared byte for byte against HEAD and identical, the runtime script tag unchanged, and both storage keys left untouched.

## 2026-08-14 Claude — overhaul campaign batch 7d: Wordweave Grid's dead-end check was measuring the wrong thing

Fourth landing of batch 7. A targeted pass, not a rewrite. The boards are sound and every authored word is genuinely traceable, so the work went into the one number that decides a board and the rule that was supposed to notice when it ran out.

- **The dead-end check compared against the wrong number.** Thread is spent and never refilled inside a board, so "thread left minus the total still owed" is provable, not a guess. The existing check compared the thread left against only the **shortest** unfound word, so a board owing 4 + 5 with 12 thread left read as perfectly fine when it was already lost. Caught in the act: at **12 thread left against 14 still owed**, the new rule declares the board finished while the old shortest-word rule still said fine. Proved rather than asserted: from that position no combination of the remaining words could be afforded.
- **Nothing showed how tight the thread was.** Every board opens with **exactly four spare thread** — measured, on all three — so a single wrong five-letter guess is most of your margin. The panel showed only `Thread 18`. A line under the status now reads `Thread left 13, still needed 9 for 2 words, spare 4.` and turns amber at two spare or less. Verified that a wrong word charges exactly its own length and that the spare figure drops by the same amount.
- **The message you actually see now carries the numbers.** Because the run ends the instant the board becomes unwinnable, the "cannot be finished" wording was never going to reach the player, so the out-of-thread message itself now reads `Out of thread: 2 words left need 14 thread and only 12 remains. Restart for a fresh run.`
- **Every authored word is checked traceable at load.** Nine words across three boards, each verified at startup to be traceable through touching cells without reusing one, and the result published. Cross-checked against an independent solver written from the posted rules: **all nine agree**, and all nine were then actually traced and accepted in play, spending exactly their own length each time, carrying the run to completion across all three boards. So the authored lists needed no widening — they needed a guard, which they now have, so a future edit to a board or a word cannot quietly ship an impossible target.
- **Storage.** `Number(read(BEST_KEY,"0")) || 0` let `Infinity` and `1e999` through, printing `Infinity` on the scoreboard and, since `JSON.stringify` writes Infinity as `null`, breaking the diagnostics contract. `1e24` printed as `1e+24`. Now finite, positive, whole and capped.
- **Findings deliberately left alone.** The game ships **three boards of three words**, which is thin for replay value, but every board is hand-authored with hidden words and adding more is content design rather than a defect fix, so I left the count alone and am naming it. Restarting does **not** discard the score here, unlike the last six games: best is written on every accepted word, so nothing is lost — checked, not assumed.
- **No timing bug, stated plainly.** The loop already consumes real elapsed time with the per-frame gap clamped, and it is already gated on the status timer: an untouched board costs **zero** animation frames once the opening line expires, and eleven seconds untouched changes nothing. Confirmed by driving 60Hz and 120Hz runs from the same synthetic clock base — the status timer behaves identically. **This game does not have the double-speed defect.**
- **One thing my own probe got wrong, worth recording.** The keyboard steps in four directions only, while the board's adjacency rule allows all eight, so my first attempt walked a diagonal solver path and traced `LXINLE` instead of `LINE`. That raised the fair question of whether a keyboard-only player is locked out of any word, so I checked it directly: **all nine words are traceable using four-direction movement alone**, so there is no parity gap. The probe now walks four-direction paths.
- Verified: a 27-assertion Playwright probe covering all of the above, plus twenty corrupt saved scores leaving a finite whole best with zero page errors, a scored word banking the best through a reload, the real capture recipe still tracing and accepting the first target word with the thread plan present, `advanceTime` byte-identical across two fresh loads, Tab moving focus off the board with nothing trapping it, and a phone check at both the opening and the **third** board with the Submit button above the fold, the plan unclipped and no sideways scroll. Plus scoped `test:games`, `capture:games` (desktop and mobile both 0), `audit:contrast` clean, catalog validation, catalog freeze, `check-corrupt-storage` across all 100 games, `npm test` 56/56, and `git diff --check` clean. Protected meta and JSON-LD blocks compared byte for byte against HEAD and identical, the runtime script tag unchanged, and both storage keys left untouched.

## 2026-08-14 Claude — overhaul campaign batch 7c: Pocket Orchard let a season become impossible and only told you on the last day

Third landing of batch 7. A targeted pass, not a rewrite. The orchard rules are sound and every season is winnable, so the work went into the thing a planning game most owes the player: whether the plan still fits the budget.

- **A season can run out of water or days, and nothing said so.** Water and growing days only ever go **down** inside a season — nothing refills the tank and nothing adds a day — so once either falls short of what the unfilled orders still need, that season is finished and the player is only told when the last day closes. Proved by draining the tank on four apple plots: from the moment the water no longer covers the waterings still owed, the panel reads `This season can no longer be finished: the tank holds 0 of the 1 waterings left to do... Restart to plan it again.` Then, as the campaign requires of any such claim, the probe **played perfectly from that warned position and still lost the season**.
- **It says what the plan actually needs, all the time.** A line under the season name now reads `Still to grow: 4 berry (2 plots, 8 cash), 2 apple (1 plot, 5 cash). Needs 7 of your 14 water and 3 of your 6 days.` — the crops still owed, the plots and cash to raise them, and the water and days they will consume against what remains. Verified against an independent re-implementation of the crop table and day rules written from the posted values: across **74 sampled states of a complete winning run, every water, day, cash and plot figure matched exactly**, and no state during a winning run was ever wrongly called dead.
- **Only the provable death is called death.** Cash and plots can both recover — a sale pays and a harvest frees a plot — so a shortage of either is reported plainly (`a sale will cover it`, `harvest first`) but never called fatal. Only water and days, the two that cannot go back up, trigger the stop message. The day case is caught too, the moment the slowest crop still owed no longer fits: `the slowest crop still owed needs 2 growing days and only 1 remain`.
- **The game stopped giving advice it knew was useless.** With the tank dry, the built-in suggestion still said "plant berry" — cash on a plot that could never be watered, so could never ripen. It now refuses to suggest a crop this season can no longer grow.
- **Restart threw the run away.** Best was only ever written when the book closed or the market shut, so restarting mid-season discarded the cash made — measured at 73 earned and nothing stored. Start now banks first.
- **Storage.** `Number(read(BEST_KEY,"0")) || 0` let `Infinity` and `1e999` through, printing `Infinity` on the scoreboard and, since `JSON.stringify` writes Infinity as `null`, breaking the diagnostics contract. `1e24` printed as `1e+24`. Now finite, positive, whole and capped.
- **No rebalance.** A player who simply follows the game's own suggestion **wins all five seasons and closes the book at 455 cash**, so the orchard is solvable and the advice is good. Nothing changed.
- **Findings deliberately left alone.** Each plot carries a `wilt` counter that increments whenever a crop goes unwatered overnight — it is published in the diagnostics but **nothing reads it**: no penalty, no display, no effect. The real punishment for neglect is already the day budget, which the new plan line now makes visible, so I left the dead counter alone rather than invent a penalty the game was never balanced around. Its loop is also **already gated** (an untouched menu costs zero animation frames and eleven seconds untouched changes nothing) and only decays the status line; it did step a fixed 1/60s per animation frame, so that line faded twice as fast on a 120Hz display, which is cosmetic rather than a gameplay bug because this orchard is turn-based. Fixed anyway, and stated plainly: **this is not a double-speed gameplay defect**.
- Verified: a 30-assertion Playwright probe covering all of the above, plus the real capture recipe still following the advice and selling an order with the plan present in the frame, **the persistence gate's own driver still reaching a failure with cash banked**, a closed market banking its cash through a clear-and-reload, twenty corrupt saved scores leaving a finite whole best with zero page errors, `advanceTime` byte-identical across two fresh loads, Tab moving focus off the board with nothing trapping it, and a phone check at both the opening and the **fifth** season with the Plant button above the fold, the plan unclipped and no sideways scroll. Plus, run in full, the **`check-best-on-fail-persistence` gate (14 games)** which drives this game, scoped `test:games`, `capture:games` (desktop and mobile both 0), `audit:contrast` clean, catalog validation, catalog freeze, `check-corrupt-storage` across all 100 games, `npm test` 56/56, and `git diff --check` clean. Protected meta and JSON-LD blocks compared byte for byte against HEAD and identical, the runtime script tag unchanged, and both storage keys left untouched so the persistence gate still matches.

## 2026-08-14 Claude — overhaul campaign batch 7b: Crown Circuit made you hunt for the three tiles you were allowed to play

Second landing of batch 7. A targeted pass, not a rewrite. The duel engine and the rival are sound and every one of the five duels is winnable, so the work went into the rule the board judged you by and never drew.

- **Three of forty-nine tiles are playable, and the board marked none of them.** Claiming needs a tile that touches your own circuit or sits one step from your crown, is not a wall, and is not under the rival crown. Measured at each duel's opening: **3, 2, 2, 3, 3 playable tiles out of 49**. The player had to walk the cursor around pressing blind, and every refusal gave the same line, `No connected circuit can reach that tile`, even when the real reason was a wall or the rival crown. Every legal tile is now outlined on the board with the exact score it pays, colour-split by what it is: a neutral claim, a rival tile taken, a crown step, a crest, or the tile that ends the duel. A strip under the board reads the cursor tile out in full: `CLAIM CREST +37 — Claims a neutral tile holding a crest for +37, and steps your crown onto it. Control 9/9, crests 2/2. That wins the duel.`
- **It cannot drift from the rules.** All of it comes from one pure `moveOutlook()` that the board overlay draws, the cursor strip reads and `playerAction()` now executes from, so the three cannot disagree. Verified against an independent re-implementation of the claim rule written from the visible rules: across all five duels, **the set of tiles marked playable and the payout printed on each matched exactly**, and every marked tile then paid precisely the number on it when claimed.
- **A refusal now says which of four things is wrong.** Wall, rival crown, your own crown, or not connected — all four found on the board and each replayed: pressing Claim on them spends **no action point, scores nothing**, and the message is the same sentence the board had already shown.
- **One of those four used to be accepted.** Claiming the tile your own crown stands on passed the old legality check, spent an action point and did **literally nothing** — the tile was already yours, the crown could not move onto itself, and the score gained zero, yet the game reported `Circuit tile claimed`. It is refused now, with the reason stated.
- **Restart threw the run away.** Best was only ever written when a duel was won or lost, so restarting mid-duel discarded everything scored — measured at 367 points earned and nothing stored. Both write paths now go through one `commitBest()` which Start calls first.
- **Storage.** `Number(read(BEST_KEY,"0")) || 0` let `Infinity` and `1e999` through, printing `Infinity` on the scoreboard and, since `JSON.stringify` writes Infinity as `null`, breaking the diagnostics contract. `1e24` printed as `1e+24`. Now finite, positive, whole and capped.
- **Two canvas lines deleted for carrying no information.** The strip under the board used to read `Target 9 control + 2 crests` and `Cursor 0,2 Recommended 4,3`. The side panel already shows control and crests with meters and states the target in the mission text, the cursor is outlined on the board and the recommendation is drawn as a dashed box — so both lines repeated what the player could already see, and they collided with the new verdict strip.
- **No rebalance.** A player who simply follows the game's own dashed recommendation **wins all five duels in six turns for 2326**, so the duels are solvable and the rival is beatable. Nothing changed.
- **Timing, honestly.** This board is turn-based, so the loop only decays the status line and a tile pulse, and it was **already gated** — an untouched menu costs zero animation frames and eleven seconds untouched changes nothing. It did step a fixed 1/60s per animation frame, so both effects faded twice as fast on a 120Hz display; that is cosmetic here rather than a gameplay bug, and it now consumes real elapsed time in fixed slices with the per-frame elapsed clamped. I am not claiming a double-speed gameplay defect in this game, because there is not one.
- Verified: a 26-assertion Playwright probe covering all of the above, plus a tile the board calls duel-winning really ending the duel, a lost duel banking its score and surviving a reload, twenty corrupt saved scores leaving a finite whole best with zero page errors, the real capture recipe still following the recommendation and taking control, `advanceTime` byte-identical across two fresh loads, Tab moving focus off the board with nothing trapping it, and a phone check at both the opening and the deepest duel with the Claim button above the fold, the status line unclipped and no sideways scroll. Plus scoped `test:games`, `capture:games` (desktop and mobile both 0), `audit:contrast` clean, catalog validation, catalog freeze, `check-corrupt-storage` across all 100 games, `npm test` 56/56, and `git diff --check` clean. Protected meta, JSON-LD, CSP and runtime blocks verified byte-identical to HEAD, and both storage keys left untouched.

## 2026-08-14 Claude — overhaul campaign batch 7a: Finale Foundry ran at double speed on a 120Hz screen

First landing of batch 7. A targeted pass, not a rewrite. This is a rhythm game, and the headline finding is a real timing defect that made it unplayable on half the monitors in the world.

- **Double speed on a 120Hz display, measured at 1.99x.** The loop was `update(1000/60)` once per animation frame — a fixed 16.67ms tick regardless of how fast the screen actually refreshes. On a 120Hz panel the foundry clock ran at exactly twice real time, so every authored spark arrived twice as fast while the strike window stayed 190ms. Proved before the fix by capturing the animation callback, letting one real frame fire so the running loop re-queued into the override, then driving 60Hz and 120Hz runs from the same synthetic clock base: **two seconds of real time produced 2017ms of game clock at 60Hz and 4017ms at 120Hz**. The loop now consumes real elapsed time in fixed 60Hz slices through an accumulator, clamped so a backgrounded tab cannot dump a huge catch-up, and the accumulator is zeroed when a run starts. `advanceTime()` still feeds the same fixed slices, so the scripted path is untouched. After the fix the two runs agree to within a millisecond.
- **The loop never slept.** `requestAnimationFrame(loop)` re-queued unconditionally, so the page burned about 60 frames a second forever — on the menu before you ever pressed Start, and after the run had ended. Measured at 48 frames per 0.8s idle on the menu and 49 after a failure. It now sleeps when nothing is moving and wakes on any action; an untouched menu costs **zero** frames once the opening line expires, and a finished run leaves it asleep. That is the seventh game in this campaign with an ungated loop.
- **Restart threw the run away.** Best was only ever written on a win or a heat collapse, so pressing Restart part way through a run discarded everything it had scored — measured at 171 points earned and nothing stored. It banks first now.
- **A rhythm game that would not tell you your timing.** A missed strike said only `Missed timing`, which teaches nothing. Every strike knows its signed distance from the beat, so it now says it: `Spark 3 fused 28ms early. +79, combo x4.` on a hit, `Missed: spark 1 struck 240ms early, outside the 190ms window.` on a mistimed strike, `Missed: spark 1 is in lane 2, the hammer is in lane 1.` on a wrong-lane strike, and `Missed: spark 3 in lane 1 went cold, never struck.` when one lapsed. The **combo reset** is named too — it is the score multiplier and a miss drops it straight back to one, which the old build never mentioned: `Combo x4 lost.`
- **Storage.** `Number(read(BEST_KEY,"0")) || 0` let `Infinity` and `1e999` through, printing `Infinity` on the scoreboard and, because `JSON.stringify` writes Infinity as `null`, breaking the diagnostics contract outright. `1e24` printed as `1e+24`. The read is now finite, positive, whole and capped.
- **Findings deliberately left alone.** On a phone the touch pad sits at 756 of 844 pixels down, below the usual two-thirds guideline — but it is **fully visible without scrolling**, it is where a thumb naturally rests, and it measured **identically at 756 on the unmodified build**, so it is a pre-existing layout choice rather than anything this pass caused. Both rendered surfaces still score 0. I left it.
- Verified: a 27-assertion Playwright probe covering all of the above, plus the published note times matching an independent reading of the authored tracks, following the game's own advice landing every spark of stage one and clearing it, the combo climbing 2, 3, 4 across consecutive hits, a losing run banking its score and surviving a reload, twenty corrupt saved scores leaving a finite whole best with zero page errors, the real capture recipe still lane-matching and striking, scripted play through `advanceTime` repeating exactly, and Tab moving focus off the board with nothing trapping it. Plus, run in full, the **`check-best-on-fail-persistence` gate (14 games)** which drives this game directly, `check-realtime-progression`, `check-corrupt-storage` across all 100 games, `check-best-score-guard`, scoped `test:games`, `capture:games` (desktop and mobile both 0), `audit:contrast` clean, catalog validation, catalog freeze, `npm test` 56/56, and `git diff --check` clean. The first scoped smoke run reported a catalog-side `Continue playing rail missing "Saved" action`, which is an index-page check unrelated to this game; three consecutive clean re-runs followed. Protected meta, JSON-LD, CSP and runtime blocks verified byte-identical to HEAD, and both storage keys left untouched so the persistence gate still matches.

## 2026-08-14 Claude — overhaul campaign batch 6f: Clause Courier never told you how many swaps you actually had left to make

Sixth and final landing of batch 6. A targeted pass, not a rewrite. The five dispatches are authored rather than generated and all five are solvable, so the work went into the number the puzzle is entirely about and never showed.

- **The one number that decides everything was missing.** Each dispatch is a pure sorting puzzle: reorder word tiles into a target phrase using adjacent swaps, inside a move budget. The exact shortest route from any arrangement is the inversion count of the rail against the target, which is cheap and exact — and the game showed none of it. It showed `Par`, a made-up target that sits **two to four swaps above the true optimum on every stage**, and a running count of words in the right place, which is the wrong measure entirely for adjacent-swap sorting. A `Swaps To Go` reading now sits in the panel and across the rail: `3 SWAPS STILL NEEDED · 7 LEFT IN BUDGET · CLEAN FINISH 905`.
- **It is exact, and proved three ways.** Checked against an independent inversion count written from the visible rules, against a **breadth-first search over the whole swap space** confirming no shorter route exists, and against actually solving each dispatch — which took **precisely the advertised number of swaps every time: 3, 7, 8, 7, 11** against pars of 5, 9, 10, 11, 13.
- **Every swap now tells you whether it was worth making.** Because a single adjacent swap moves the count by exactly one in one direction or the other, the rail can always judge it: `Good swap. 2 more needed, 8 moves left. Finish clean for 1195.` or `Wasted swap, that went the wrong way. 3 more needed, 7 moves left. Finish clean for 905.` Verified across every swap of all five solves that a productive swap lowers the reading by exactly one, and that a deliberately wasteful one raises it by exactly one and is named as such.
- **A dispatch could become unwinnable in silence.** Waste enough moves and the budget can no longer cover the swaps still required — the stage is already lost, but the old build let you keep swapping until the budget ran dry with no hint. The rail now says so the moment it happens: `7 swaps still needed but only 5 moves left. Restart this dispatch.` Proved true rather than assumed: from the warned position the probe **played perfectly and still ran out of budget**, and the independent count agrees the position was already lost.
- **The scoring was five invisible terms.** The payout is a base, plus 55 for each unspent move, plus 90 for each swap under par, minus 20 for each over, plus a stage bonus, floored — none of it visible. It is now one function that both the real award and the preview run through, so `Finish clean for 1195` cannot drift from what finishing actually pays. Checked against an independent scoring: all five stage payouts match exactly, and a perfect run totals **6615**.
- **No rebalance.** Every dispatch is solvable, the shortest routes are 3, 7, 8, 7 and 11 against budgets of 9, 13, 14, 15 and 17, so there is real slack for mistakes and the difficulty is already fair. Nothing changed.
- **Storage.** `Number(value.bestScore) || 0` let `{"bestScore":1e400}` through as Infinity, which printed `Infinity` on the scoreboard and, because `JSON.stringify` writes Infinity as `null`, broke the diagnostics contract outright. `-5` showed a negative best and `1e24` printed as `1e+24`. Both stored fields are now finite, positive, whole and capped.
- **Findings deliberately left alone.** `state.flash` is set on every swap and decayed inside `advanceTime`, but **nothing reads it** — not the canvas, not the diagnostics — so it has no observable effect and I did not add machinery to service it. The canvas also repeats the status line word for word below the tiles; that duplication is pre-existing, and every alternative I considered either lost the message in fullscreen or duplicated something else instead, so I left it and am naming it rather than fiddling. Best-on-abandon is genuinely fine here, unlike the last three games: the score is banked at each dispatch completion, and restarting a dispatch restores the score to its value at the start of that dispatch.
- **No timing bugs here, stated plainly.** This game contains zero `requestAnimationFrame` calls, so there is no double-speed bug and nothing to put to sleep. Twelve seconds untouched changes nothing and costs zero animation frames.
- Verified: a 33-assertion Playwright probe covering all of the above, plus twenty corrupt saved stats leaving a sensible scoreboard and valid diagnostics with zero page errors, restarting a dispatch keeping the score banked from earlier ones, the real capture recipe still swapping along the rail and landing on a live frame carrying the new reading, `advanceTime` byte-identical across two fresh loads, Tab moving focus off the board with nothing trapping it, and a phone check at both the opening state **and the seven-tile final dispatch** confirming the tiles and the Select button stay above the fold with no sideways scroll. Plus scoped `test:games`, `capture:games` (desktop and mobile both 0), `audit:contrast` clean, catalog validation, catalog freeze, `check-corrupt-storage` across all 100 games, `npm test` 56/56, and `git diff --check` clean. The first corrupt-storage run reported a transport `ERR_NO_BUFFER_SPACE` on an untouched game; a clean re-run passed all 100. Protected meta, JSON-LD, CSP and runtime blocks verified byte-identical to HEAD, and both storage keys left untouched.

## 2026-08-14 Claude — overhaul campaign batch 6e: Echo Mimic hid your place in the sequence, hid the speed-up that kills every run, and had a Start button that did nothing

Fifth landing of batch 6. A targeted pass, not a rewrite. Five other gates drive this page, so every change was made to keep their contracts intact and all five were re-run in full.

- **You could not see where you were.** Four pads flash, you repeat them, and the only guide was a status line. During playback there was no position at all, so on a nine-step sequence there was nothing to count against. A twelve-dot track now sits under the status: while the sequence plays it lights the step currently flashing, while you repeat it fills in behind you and marks the step it wants next. It is **position only and never carries a colour while a run is live**, so it aids counting without giving the answer away. Proved by stepping the clock through a whole playback and reading the row: markers advance 0, 1, 2, 3 in order with exactly one live marker at a time, then fill in one per correct tap.
- **The speed-up was completely invisible, and it is what ends every run.** Each round the flash time drops by 18ms from 420ms until it floors at 220ms, so by round twelve the sequence plays at roughly half its opening pace. Nothing said so. A pace readout now sits beside the track reading `Flash 330ms`, turning amber at `Flash 220ms (top speed)`, and the opening announcement of each round reads `Watch the sequence. 6 steps at 330ms.` Checked against an independent re-implementation of the posted rule across a real fourteen-round run: **420, 402, 384, 366, 348, 330, 312, 294, 276, 258, 240, 222, 220, 220**, every one matching.
- **Losing told you nothing.** The run ended on `Wrong pad!` and a round count, with no idea which step you fluffed or what the sequence even was. The card now reads `Step 3 of 6: you pressed yellow, it was red. Here is the whole sequence.` followed by the full sequence as coloured dots with a red ring on the step you missed. Verified by deliberately missing a known step and checking the recorded step index, the pad pressed and the pad expected all match, and that exactly one dot carries the miss marker and it is the right one.
- **The Start button did nothing at all during a run.** Confirmed on the old build in both live phases: pressed during playback and again while repeating, nothing changed and no message appeared. There was also no way out of a run except deliberately hitting a wrong pad. The button now reads `Stop` while a run is live and ends it properly, which is the path `finishRun(true)` was already written for and which nothing ever reached — its `Run Stopped` title was dead code. A stopped run banks the round it reached.
- **A real timing defect: the clock was split in two.** The next round was scheduled on a real `setTimeout`, so `advanceTime()` could not cross a round boundary at all — a scripted run stuck on round one forever, which is exactly what my probe hit. The wait now runs on the game's own clock in a short `gap` phase the tick loop drives, so `advanceTime` drives a whole run end to end. That meant the loop had to stay awake through the gap, which introduced a **double-rAF hazard**: playback can now start the next round from inside `tick`, and the round setup re-arms the loop itself, so `tick` would have armed a second chain. Guarded. An idle board still costs zero animation frames and a stopped run leaves the loop asleep, both measured.
- **The stored best was unguarded.** `{"round":1e400}` parses to Infinity, passes a bare `typeof === 'number'` check, prints `Infinity` on the scoreboard and — worse — `JSON.stringify` writes it as `null`, so the diagnostics contract broke outright. `-5` showed a negative best and `1e24` printed as `1e+24`. The read is now finite, whole, at least 1 and capped, and the stored length is clamped too.
- **Findings deliberately left alone.** The reduced-motion gate names this page as its host, but it builds and measures **its own probe element** rather than anything belonging to the game, so no element here had to be preserved — worth recording, because the campaign note assumed otherwise. This game is also not on that gate's motion-aware list and has no decorative canvas motion to gate, so I did not add a `reducedMotion` flag it would not use. And event ages in the diagnostics are computed from a clock that only ticks while a run is live, so they can read stale in `over`; nothing player-facing reads them and making the clock free-running would make captured frames time-dependent.
- Verified: a 30-assertion Playwright probe covering all of the above, plus twenty corrupt saved bests leaving a sensible scoreboard and valid diagnostics with zero page errors, a losing run's best surviving a clear-and-reload, scripted play through `advanceTime` repeating exactly, Tab moving focus normally with nothing trapping it, the number keys still playing a pad, the real capture recipe still reaching a correct-event frame, and a phone check at eleven steps confirming the track wraps without pushing the pads below the fold or scrolling sideways. Plus, run in full, **every gate that drives this page**: `check-reduced-motion` (35 games), `check-pwa-runtime` (offline replay of this page), `check-runtime-storage-behavior` (opaque sandboxed frame), `check-player-storage-bridge` (seed, write-behind, scoped clear, reload persistence), and `check-scoped-verification` (which runs scoped smoke and capture over exactly this slug). Plus scoped `test:games`, `capture:games` (desktop and mobile both 0), `audit:contrast` clean, catalog validation, catalog freeze, `check-corrupt-storage` across all 100 games, `npm test` 56/56, and `git diff --check` clean. Protected meta, JSON-LD, CSP and runtime blocks verified byte-identical to HEAD, and both storage keys left untouched so the bridge gate still matches.

## 2026-08-14 Claude — overhaul campaign batch 6d: Dice Dynamo's only real decision was a blind coin flip

Fourth landing of batch 6. A targeted pass, not a rewrite. The dice rules were correct and the scoring was already side-effect free, so the work went into the one decision the game actually asks you to make.

- **The Risk button was the whole game, and it told you nothing.** Every round you lock dice and choose whether to reroll. The screen showed what banking pays right now and nothing whatsoever about what a reroll is worth — not the odds, not the spread, and no warning that rerolling can throw away a contract that is already live. The panel now prices it exactly: `A reroll keeps High Voltage 63% of the time. Rerolling then banking averages 74 against 66 now, anywhere from 13 to 128.` with a headline `Risk +8` in green when rerolling gains on average and red when it costs. When the contract is live and a reroll could lose it the line leads with `Risks High Voltage` in amber.
- **The odds are counted, not sampled.** `facts()` and `quote()` now take the hand to judge instead of reading the table directly, which let `rerollOutlook()` enumerate **every** way the unlocked dice could land — up to 6^5 = 7776 outcomes — and price each one through the *same* `quote()` the Bank button runs. The reading therefore cannot disagree with what banking then pays. Proved by re-implementing the scoring independently from the posted rules in the probe and comparing: **36 sampled states across every lock size from none locked to four locked, all five outcome counts (6, 36, 216, 1296, 7776), zero mismatches on outcomes, keeps, chance, average, best and worst.**
- **The danger warning is real, and it is worth 28 points.** The probe searched the deterministic state space for a hand where the board calls a reroll dangerous, then **replayed that exact line on a fresh page**: a live High Voltage contract paying 66 to bank, warned at 26% survival, rerolled, contract lost, payout down to 38. Independently re-counted; the 26% is right.
- **No rebalance, and here is the arithmetic.** I brute-forced the fixed seed offline with a 400-wide beam search over every lock-set pair per round: never rerolling tops out at **121**, a mechanical player that follows the new advice with no lock skill reaches **162**, and skilled locking reaches **771 with five of the six contracts hit**. The ceiling is more than six times the floor and every bit of that gap is the lock choice, so the targets are already fair. Left alone.
- **Two storage defects.** `Number(read(BEST_KEY,"0")) || 0` let `Infinity` and `1e999` through as Infinity — which `JSON.stringify` writes as `null`, breaking the diagnostics contract outright — and let `-5` through as a negative best. My first guard checked only finite-and-positive, and the probe's twenty-seed battery immediately caught `999999999999999999999999` sailing through as a finite `1e24` that printed on the scoreboard as `1e+24`; the read is now finite, positive, whole and capped. Separately, best was only ever written when a run **finished**, so restarting part way through discarded everything banked that run. `newRun()` banks first now.
- **The shared gate got stronger for everyone.** `check-best-score-guard.mjs` only ever seeded one corrupt value (`not-a-number-xyz`), which would never have caught any of the above. It now seeds four (`not-a-number-xyz`, `Infinity`, `1e999`, `-1`) and lists this game as a fourth entry. All four listed games pass all four values, so this was a free catalog-wide strengthening rather than a new burden.
- **Findings deliberately left alone.** The footer message carries a duration that is only ever decremented inside `advanceTime`, so in real play a message never reverts to the default prompt — every action replaces it anyway, and adding a real timer would make the captured frames and the diagnostics time-dependent for a purely cosmetic gain. The desktop layout also leaves a lot of empty space above the table because the app grid centres it; pre-existing, and both rendered surfaces still score 0.
- **No timing bugs here, stated plainly.** This game contains zero `requestAnimationFrame` calls, so there is no double-speed bug, nothing to put to sleep, and no auto-start: eleven seconds untouched changes nothing and costs zero animation frames.
- Verified: a 30-assertion Playwright probe covering all of the above, plus the two dead states of the Risk button both explained before you press it (every die locked, and no rerolls left, the latter with the button disabled), a full six-round run that finishes and stores its score, following the advice beating never rerolling, twenty corrupt seeds leaving a finite whole best on screen *and* in the diagnostics with zero page errors, the real capture recipe still locking a die and banking into round two with the priced reroll line present, `advanceTime` byte-identical across two fresh loads, Tab moving focus normally with nothing trapping it, the posted keys locking, risking and banking, and a phone check for the Bank button above the fold, an unclipped reroll line and no sideways scroll. Plus the **full `check-best-score-guard` gate**, `check-corrupt-storage` (all 100 games), scoped `test:games`, `capture:games` (desktop and mobile both 0), `audit:contrast` clean, catalog validation, catalog freeze, `npm test` 56/56, and `git diff --check` clean. Protected meta, JSON-LD, CSP and runtime blocks verified byte-identical to HEAD.

## 2026-08-14 Claude — overhaul campaign batch 6c: Gridline Tactics hid every rule it judges you by, and trapped keyboard focus on the board

Third landing of batch 6. A targeted readability and accessibility pass, not a rewrite — the tactics engine underneath was sound, and the audit found no timing or auto-start bugs to invent.

- **Four in five squares were a silent dead press.** Move range 3, shot range 2, smash-a-crate range 1, the crate-in-the-way rule that drops a shot from 3 to 2, the crate-beside-you rule that halves an incoming hit, and the mission bonus that pays 12 per HP still standing — every one of them was invisible, with no square highlighting of any kind. Measured on mission one: **35 of 42 cursor squares do nothing**, refusing for five different reasons (28 out of move range, 3 crate out of reach, 2 shot out of range, 1 occupied, 1 the unit's own square), and all the player ever got back was `no line`. The board now washes every reachable square teal, rings every enemy the selected unit can shoot with the exact damage or `KO`, outlines every crate in reach, and carries a bar above the grid reading `SHOOT E1 -2` with `A crate in the way drops the shot from 3 to 2. E1 drops to 2 HP for +20.` Refusals are explained too: `OUT OF RANGE - E1 is 4 squares away and your shot reaches 2.` `Cursor` in the status panel repeats the verdict at page text size, which is what a phone can actually read.
- **The warning cannot drift from the rule.** Every tactical number moved into one block of constants that `actionPlan()`, `playerAction()`, `enemyTurn()` and `checkWin()` all read, and `playerAction()` now resolves exactly the plan the board just advertised instead of re-deriving its own. The probe sweeps all 42 squares, presses Action on each from an identical fresh mission, and checks the outcome against what was advertised: **every move square moves the unit there for the advertised score, every refused square changes nothing at all, and across a full five-mission run 29 shots each stripped exactly the promised damage for exactly the promised score.**
- **The danger numbers are a replay, not a guess.** First attempt marked squares next to a live enemy and printed one enemy's damage. The probe caught it immediately: units took 3 and 6 where it promised 2, because several enemies can strike the same unit in one phase. Replaced with `previewEnemyPhase()`, which replays the enemy turn step for step on throwaway copies with the unit relocated, and reports what that unit would actually take. The board now prints `-2` or an amber `-1` on each standable square and says whether the reading is the whole phase (`That is the whole enemy phase.`) or still a forecast (`Your other unit could still pull them away.`). Proved by moving the **last** unit to act onto a square the board calls certain and checking the phase deals precisely that: 5 for 5, including the halved hits and the one that reads `and unit B goes down`.
- **A real keyboard trap, and the obvious fix was not enough.** The board called `preventDefault()` on Tab *and* Shift+Tab, so four real Tab presses never moved focus off the canvas. I first copied the sibling game's fix — take plain Tab, let Shift+Tab escape — and the probe proved that still leaves every button unreachable, because Restart, Sound, Fullscreen and all eight touch buttons sit **after** the canvas in the page, so forward Tab is the only way to them. Tab is now handed back to the browser completely and `Q` switches unit instead, matching the on-screen Unit button. Verified: tabbing off the board now lands on Restart and then Sound.
- **Score was thrown away on restart.** Best was only saved on a win or a loss, so hitting Restart mid-mission discarded everything earned since the last mission ended. `restart()` banks first now; verified against a cleared store.
- **Findings deliberately left alone.** The rising `move` and `-2` flourishes clip a unit's letter for about seven tenths of a second as they drift; cosmetic and reduced-motion already suppresses them. Danger numbers are still drawn on squares the selected unit cannot reach this turn — that is intentional, it maps where the enemy holds ground for next turn. And I did **not** rebalance: a greedy player following only what the board now says clears all five missions for **3700**, so the targets are already fair and the HP-scaled mission bonus already rewards playing carefully.
- **No timing bugs here, stated plainly.** The only animation loop drives the decorative floats and is already gated to `state.feedback.length > 0`; an untouched board burns zero frames and eleven seconds of thinking time changes nothing. The float step is thresholded on real elapsed wall-clock rather than counted per frame, so 60Hz and 120Hz runs decay identically — measured, not assumed.
- Verified: a 35-assertion Playwright probe covering all of the above plus 20 corrupt-storage seeds leaving a finite best with zero page errors, `advanceTime` byte-identical across two fresh loads, the real capture recipe still landing on a live informative frame, the verdict bar confirmed painted in the move colour and confirmed carrying none of it when refused, and a phone check for the action button above the fold, an unclipped verdict pill and no sideways scroll. Plus the **full `check-reduced-motion` gate (35 games)**, which lists this game, scoped `test:games`, `capture:games` (desktop and mobile both 0), `audit:contrast` clean, catalog validation, catalog freeze, `check-best-score-guard`, `check-corrupt-storage` (all 100 games), `npm test` 56/56, and `git diff --check` clean. ## 2026-08-14 Claude — overhaul campaign batch 6b: Moonbase Mutex let you commit a move that killed the run, silently

Second landing of batch 6. One readability defect with fatal consequences, and an honest note that the usual timing bugs are simply not present here.

- **Four ways to die, none of them checked before you committed.** You queue a move for every crew and then run the tick. That tick ends the shift outright if any crew walks into a bulkhead or off the grid, if any crew enters an airlock that is closed this parity, if two crews target the same cell, or if two crews swap through a hatch. All four were only ever evaluated at the instant of commit, so the player had to hold four simultaneous-movement rules in their head across every crew and got no warning at all — just an immediate `Mutex Fault` and a dead run. The board now ghosts each crew's queued destination, outlines it green when legal and red when not, and carries a bar that either reads `Safe tick: +8 score, 7 air left` or `RUN TICK ENDS THE SHIFT - Crew A would hit a sealed bulkhead`. The status panel echoes the verdict as `Safe: +8` or `STOP: wall`, which is what stays readable on a phone.
- **The warning cannot be wrong.** All four checks plus the safe-case payout were extracted into one pure `previewTick()`, and `runTick()` now executes from it rather than re-deriving them — the original failure messages and event names are preserved exactly so the shared best-on-fail driver is unaffected. The probe sweeps every command pair at several depths to find each verdict the preview can produce, then replays each one on a fresh page and asserts the real tick ends the shift with that exact event: **all four faults confirmed, plus a safe tick that pays precisely the advertised score**.
- **Storage guard.** A stored best of `Infinity` or `1e999` survived the old `Number(read(...)) || 0`; the read is now finite-checked.
- **No timing bugs here, stated plainly.** This game has no animation loop at all — zero `requestAnimationFrame` calls — so there is no double-speed bug and nothing to put to sleep, and the board never spins a frame. The usual class-three trap (state that only decays inside `advanceTime`) does not bite either: `feedbackMs` is decremented by `advanceTime` but is read by nothing — not the canvas, not the diagnostics — so it has no observable effect. I left it alone rather than add a loop to service a value nobody reads.
- **Auto-start audit: clean.** Three seconds untouched leaves the mode, tick and score at zero.
- Verified: a 21-assertion Playwright probe — the menu is inert and the board never runs an animation loop; the preview is published while playing; **every verdict it can name is replayed on a fresh page and confirmed to end the shift with that exact event, and a safe tick pays exactly the advertised gain**; `advanceTime` is byte-identical across two fresh loads; the real capture recipe still resolves its ticks; the danger warning is confirmed painted red by canvas pixel sampling; a cleared-and-reloaded run that burns its air fails with the score banked and saved; the phone keeps its primary action above the fold, shows the verdict at full size, and has no horizontal overflow; 20 corrupt-storage seeds leave a finite best with zero page errors. Plus the **full `check-best-on-fail-persistence` gate (14 games)**, which lists this game, scoped `test:games`, `capture:games` (desktop and mobile both 0), `audit:contrast` clean, all the static gates, `npm test` 56/56, and `git diff --check` clean. Protected meta, JSON-LD, CSP and runtime blocks verified byte-identical to HEAD.

## 2026-08-14 Claude — overhaul campaign batch 6a: Comet Cartel ran at double speed and hid the whole shape of the chase

First landing of batch 6. One real bug and a cluster of invisible rules that decide every run — a targeted pass, not a rewrite.

- **The chase ran at double speed on a 120Hz display.** The loop stepped a fixed slice per animation frame. Driving the game's own loop from a synthetic clock over the same two seconds of wall-clock, the run covered **0.222 of the route at 60Hz and 0.442 at 120Hz** — exactly double. Since the lead runner escapes at a fixed distance, that halves the real time available to catch it. The loop now consumes real elapsed time in fixed slices through an accumulator; the same measurement now reads 0.232 against 0.232. `advanceTime` keeps its own untouched fixed-step path, and the loop already slept correctly on the menu.
- **The one readout that existed was clamped exactly where it mattered.** The board printed `distance NN%` as `Math.min(1, distance) * 100`, but the chase actually runs to **1.42**, and the lead only becomes attackable past **0.68**. So the number pinned at 100% for the entire endgame — the whole stretch between closing on the lead and losing it — and never moved again. It is replaced by a chase bar drawn over the true range with the lead-in-range point marked in amber and the escape line marked in red, so the player can see where they are, when they can start hurting the lead, and how much chase is left.
- **Firing had three invisible conditions.** A shot only reaches the lead runner when the intel quota is met **and** the chase has closed past 0.68; otherwise it merely tags whatever escort shares the lane. On top of that, firing is silently refused when the disruptor is above a heat limit or still on cooldown. None of that was shown, so a player could press fire repeatedly at the lead and see nothing happen with no explanation. A panel now reads `NEED INTEL 1/2`, `CLOSING IN`, `OVERHEATED` or `LEAD IN RANGE`, with a line saying why, and a heat gauge sits under it. It comes from a new pure `shotPlan()` that `fire()` and the guidance target both run from, so the panel and the weapon cannot disagree.
- **Storage guard.** A stored best of `Infinity` or `1e999` survived the old `Number(read(...)) || 0`; the read is now finite-checked.
- **Auto-start audit: clean.** Twelve seconds on the menu with no input changes nothing, and the loop already slept there.
- **One probe assertion I deliberately loosened.** My copy of the capture recipe reached distance 0.47 but happened to collect no intel, while the real harness run of the same recipe scored 45 with an intel packet recovered on screen — the recipe's exact outcome is sampling-sensitive. Rather than tune my copy until it matched, the assertion now checks what the recipe is actually for: that it drives a live chase to a representative frame in a valid state. The capture harness run remains the authority on the screenshot, and it scored 0 issues on both viewports.
- Verified: a 20-assertion Playwright probe — the menu costs zero animation frames; **the chase covers the same ground in the same real time at 60Hz and 120Hz while the 120Hz run genuinely renders twice the frames**; a shot the panel says cannot reach the lead leaves its armor untouched and the panel names the missing condition, while a shot it says will reach does strip exactly one armor; letting the chase run out ends the run at the escape line the bar marks; `advanceTime` is byte-identical across two fresh loads; the real capture recipe still drives a live chase; the chase bar is confirmed painted by canvas pixel sampling on a fresh page; a cleared-and-reloaded run that never disables the lead fails with its score banked and saved; the phone keeps its primary action above the fold and has no horizontal overflow; 20 corrupt-storage seeds leave a finite best with zero page errors. Plus the **full `check-best-on-fail-persistence` gate (14 games)** and the **full `check-reduced-motion` gate (35 games)**, both of which list this game, scoped `test:games`, `capture:games` (desktop and mobile both 0), `audit:contrast` clean, the other static gates, `npm test` 56/56, and `git diff --check` clean. ## 2026-08-14 Claude — overhaul campaign batch 5f: Stack Tide hid the four pixels the whole game turns on

Sixth and final landing of batch 5. One real bug and one hidden rule that decides every run — a targeted pass, not a rewrite.

- **The rule nobody could see.** A drop counts as perfect only when the sliding platform's left edge lands within **4 pixels** of the block below, and only a perfect drop **grows the tower back**, by up to 18 pixels. Everything else trims the block to the overlap, so without perfect drops the tower narrows monotonically until a miss ends the run. That 4-pixel window, and the fact that hitting it is the only way to recover width, were drawn nowhere and stated nowhere. The board now marks the target on the block below — a short band spanning the tolerance either side of its left edge, with a matching tick on the sliding platform — and a readout above the tower reads `OFF BY 37px` or `PERFECT LINE UP`, with a line underneath saying what the drop will actually do: grow the block and by how much, trim it to a stated width, or miss entirely and end the run.
- **The guide cannot lie.** The overlap, the delta, the perfect test and the resulting width were pulled into one pure `dropAlignment()`, and `dropPlatform()` now runs from it. The probe drops four times mid-run and asserts the advertised delta, perfect flag and resulting width equal what the drop records, then deliberately lines up inside the window and asserts the block grows by exactly the advertised reward and the combo rises.
- **The loop never stopped.** `requestAnimationFrame(frame)` was re-queued unconditionally, so the page stepped, rendered and refreshed the whole HUD every frame for ever, including on the ready screen and after a run ended — 43 frames per 700ms. It now sleeps unless a run is live, shavings are still falling, the flash is fading or the camera is still settling, and wakes on a new run and on visibility change. Timing itself was already correct: the frame used real elapsed seconds clamped at 50ms, so there was no double-speed bug here.
- **Storage guard.** A stored best of `Infinity` or `1e999` survived the old `Number(safeGet(...)) || 0`; the read is now finite-checked.
- **Auto-start audit: clean.** Twelve seconds on the ready screen with no input changes nothing.
- **A mistake I made and caught.** My first edit replaced the perfect test inside the new `dropAlignment()` instead of the one inside `dropPlatform()`, leaving `dropAlignment` referencing an undefined variable. The capture harness caught it immediately with a page error and a render score of 405, and it was repaired before anything else ran. Worth recording because the two lines were textually identical — when a helper is extracted from a function, the original text now appears twice.
- **Three probe faults worth recording, not game faults.** The game listens for `event.code`, so a synthetic key with only `key: "r"` never restarted anything and a whole sub-test silently measured a dead page. A miss leaves no drop record, so comparing one is meaningless. And a pixel comparison on a page that earlier sub-tests had already driven was reading a stale frame — the fresh-page rule applies to canvas sampling too, not just key handlers.
- Verified: a 19-assertion Playwright probe — the ready screen costs zero animation frames and changes nothing over time; the guide matches the drop it describes across four drops; a drop the guide calls a miss really does end the run; lining up inside the window is a perfect drop that grows the block by exactly the advertised reward and raises the combo; a run can still be lost and the loop then sleeps; `advanceTime` is byte-identical across two fresh loads; **the real capture recipe still lands a stacked block**; the readout is confirmed painted by counting near-white pixels in its band on a fresh page; the phone keeps its drop button above the fold, starts a run on a tap, and has no horizontal overflow; 20 corrupt-storage seeds leave a finite best with zero page errors. Plus the **full `check-reduced-motion` gate (35 games)**, which lists this game, scoped `test:games`, `capture:games` (desktop and mobile both 0), `audit:contrast` clean, the other static gates, `npm test` 56/56, and `git diff --check` clean. Protected meta, JSON-LD, CSP and runtime blocks verified byte-identical to HEAD.

**Batch 5 is complete**: signal-loom, drift-loom, tempo-tunnels, forge-freighter, service-shift, stack-tide. Thirty-four of the hundred games have now been rebuilt or repaired.

## 2026-08-14 Claude — overhaul campaign batch 5e: Service Shift lost the whole run in 23 seconds while you read

Fifth landing of batch 5. Two real bugs plus a hidden scoring rule surfaced — a targeted pass, not a rewrite.

- **The shift ran from page load and lost itself in 23 seconds.** Customers began queueing the instant the page opened, each with a draining patience bar, and three walkouts end the run. A probe that loads the page and polls without pressing anything reports `game over, misses 3, served 0, score 0` after **23.0 seconds**. Reading the control hints under the board takes longer than that. This is the same defect class as Diamond Derby and it is the worst kind, because the player loses before they have made a single decision. The shift now waits: the board shows `SHIFT READY` with the line "customers only start waiting once you do", and the doors open on the first gameplay input — a station key, an arrow, Space, Enter, or a tap on a station. The sound and fullscreen buttons deliberately do not open it, which the probe checks.
- **`advanceTime` opens the shift too, and that is deliberate.** The capture recipe's first act is `advanceTime(640)` and it then reads the first order, so a gate that only opened on human input would have left the harness staring at an empty queue. Treating an explicit "advance the world by 640ms" as opening the doors keeps the recipe working exactly as written — it still prepares and serves an order, verified — while a real player, who never calls `advanceTime`, loses nothing while reading.
- **The loop never stopped.** `requestAnimationFrame(frame)` was re-queued unconditionally, so the page kept stepping and repainting for ever, including before the shift opened and after it had ended — 43 frames per 700ms. It now sleeps unless the shift is live, a banner is fading or particles are still on screen, and wakes on input and on visibility change. The timing itself was already correct here: `step()` already consumed real elapsed milliseconds through a fixed-tick accumulator, so there was no double-speed bug to fix.
- **A real determinism leak, found by the probe.** That same accumulator kept its fractional remainder from however long the page had been sitting open, so an identical `advanceTime` sequence could land one tick apart between loads — 4.333 seconds against 4.350. Opening the shift now zeroes the accumulator and the clock, and the same sequence is byte-identical across two fresh loads.
- **The scoring rule that drives everything was invisible.** Serving pays `item value + up to 70 for how much patience is left + 12 per combo step`, so a fresh order can be worth nearly twice a stale one — that freshness pressure is the entire game and nothing said so. A ready station now reads `SERVE NOW +184` with the breakdown `115 + 56 fresh + 12 combo` beneath it, from a new pure `servePayout()`. The probe serves three orders, including one deliberately left to go stale, and asserts the advertised total is exactly the score gained every time and that the freshness part really does shrink while an order waits.
- **Storage guard.** A stored best of `Infinity` or `1e999` survived the old `Number(safe.get(...)) || 0`; the read is now finite-checked.
- **Two probe faults worth recording, not game faults.** The idle-frame measurement after a loss fired while the end-of-shift banner was still legitimately animating out, and the canvas pixel sample used screen coordinates where canvas coordinates were needed. Both corrected; the second now samples the station-card band and compares preparing against ready.
- Verified: a 20-assertion Playwright probe — twenty-five seconds untouched costs nothing and zero animation frames; the sound button does not open the shift but a station key does; the advertised payout equals the score gained across fresh and stale serves and splits into base, freshness and combo; ignoring customers still loses the shift and the loop then sleeps; `advanceTime` is byte-identical across two fresh loads; **the real capture recipe still serves an order**; the ready payout is confirmed painted into the station-card band by canvas pixel sampling; the phone keeps its primary action above the fold, opens the shift on a tap, and has no horizontal overflow; 20 corrupt-storage seeds leave a finite best with zero page errors. Plus the **full `check-reduced-motion` gate (35 games)**, which lists this game, scoped `test:games`, `capture:games` (desktop and mobile both 0), `audit:contrast` clean, the other static gates, `npm test` 56/56, and `git diff --check` clean. Protected meta, JSON-LD, CSP and runtime blocks verified byte-identical to HEAD.

## 2026-08-14 Claude — overhaul campaign batch 5d: Forge Freighter made you launch blind

Fourth landing of batch 5. The headline is a readability defect with real teeth, plus two small real bugs — a targeted pass, not a rewrite.

- **Launching was a one-way bet with the number hidden.** A route pays `cargo value + destination bonuses - per-bay overload penalties - toll`, and if that total lands under the manifest quota the run ends outright — no retry, no partial credit. That total was computed only inside the launch itself. Before committing, the diagnostics carried nothing but the running profit and the quota, and the board showed neither. So the player had to hold four separate rules in their head, including a toll and per-unit penalty rates that appear nowhere in the game, and then gamble the whole run on the arithmetic. The board now carries a strip along the bottom breaking the launch into its four parts and stating the total against the quota, ending in `CLEARS` or `SHORT BY n`, coloured green or red. It reads the existing `scoreLaunch()` — which was already a pure function — so the preview and the launch are literally the same code.
- **Two status pills were actively misleading.** The header showed total Weight and total Heat summed across all three bays, but penalties are charged **per bay** against that bay's own caps, so the totals could look comfortable while one bay was badly overloaded. Per-bay load was already visible twice over, in the bay list and as canvas gauges. Those two pills are now `This launch` and `Vs quota`, which is the number the decision actually turns on and which stays readable on a phone.
- **The launch record was wiped exactly when the launch succeeded.** `loadManifest()` cleared `state.lastLaunch`, and it runs immediately after a route clears, so the diagnostic that reports the last launch was null for the most common outcome and only survived on a failure or a win. The reset moved to the start of a run, where a genuinely fresh book has no last launch. No gate depended on it; it was simply wrong.
- **The loop stepped a fixed slice per frame.** Honestly, this one is minor here: the only time-driven state is the status banner, so the effect was that the banner cleared at double speed on a 120Hz display. Fixed the same way as the rest of the campaign — real elapsed time consumed in fixed slices, with `advanceTime` left on its own fixed-step path.
- **Storage guard.** A stored best of `Infinity` or `1e999` survived the old `Number(read(...)) || 0`; the read is now finite-checked.
- **Confirmed every quota is fair before touching anything.** Brute-forcing all four-way cargo placements for each of the five manifests (1024 to 4096 combinations each) shows every quota is reachable with a wide margin — best possible 214 against a quota of 132, up to 330 against 208 — and the game's own published plan clears all five. So no rebalancing was warranted, and the defect really was that the number was hidden rather than that it was harsh.
- **Auto-start audit: clean.** Eleven seconds on the menu with no input changes nothing, and the loop already slept there.
- Verified: a 22-assertion Playwright probe — the menu costs zero frames once the banner clears; the payout is published before launching and an empty hold is previewed as far short; loading each item moves the previewed payout; **every part of the preview — value, bonus, penalties and toll — equals what the launch records, and a clearing launch banks exactly the previewed amount**; a launch the preview calls short really does end the run; following the published plan docks the freighter and saves the profit; **the strip is confirmed red while short and green once clearing by sampling the canvas pixels**; `advanceTime` is byte-identical across two fresh loads; the real capture recipe still loads and launches; the phone keeps its primary action above the fold, shows both new readouts at full size, and has no horizontal overflow; 20 corrupt-storage seeds leave a finite best with zero page errors. Plus scoped `test:games`, `capture:games` (desktop and mobile both 0), `audit:contrast` clean, all the static gates, `npm test` 56/56, and `git diff --check` clean. ## 2026-08-13 Claude — overhaul campaign batch 5c: Tempo Tunnels played at double tempo and never drew its hit window

Third landing of batch 5. Two real bugs in a single line of code, plus the missing half of the scoring rule — a targeted pass, not a rewrite.

- **The song played at exactly double tempo on a 120Hz display.** The whole loop was one line: `function loop(){update(1/60);updateUI();render();raf=requestAnimationFrame(loop)}`. Stepping a fixed 1/60 per animation frame means the track clock advances twice as fast at 120Hz — measured directly by driving the game's own loop from a synthetic clock: after two seconds of wall-clock the clock read **2016ms at 60Hz and 4016ms at 120Hz**, beat 3.9 against beat 7.8. In a rhythm game that is the whole game, and it also halves the real-time width of the 190ms hit window. The loop now consumes real elapsed time in fixed slices through an accumulator; the same measurement now reads 2100ms against 2116ms.
- **That same line never stopped.** The `requestAnimationFrame` call was unconditional, so the page ran a full update, UI sync and canvas repaint every frame forever, including sitting on the menu and after the track had failed — 42 frames per 700ms. It now sleeps once play is over and the status banner has cleared, and wakes on Start, on a new stage, and on visibility change. `advanceTime` keeps its own untouched fixed-step path.
- **The hit window was never drawn.** A sync only lands when two things are true: the nearest note is within 190ms of the gate **and** the runner is in that note's lane. The lane half was visible — notes ride their lane and the runner sits in one. The timing half was drawn nowhere at all: just a one-pixel gate line, with the window existing only as a number in a side readout. The band is now drawn around the gate, sized from the same window constant and the same scroll rate that positions the notes, so it cannot drift from the rule. The probe confirms a note the rule calls in-window is drawn inside the band, and samples the canvas pixels to confirm the band is actually lit.
- **Show the consequence before you commit.** A verdict chip now reads READY, WRONG LANE, TOO EARLY or TOO LATE for the beat you are approaching, with a one-line instruction under it, and the runner is ringed in the matching colour. It comes from a new pure `syncOutcome()` that `sync()` itself executes from. The probe drives all three situations and asserts a READY verdict really does score the note for exactly the advertised amount, and that a WRONG LANE or offbeat verdict costs exactly the integrity it advertised. A Sync-now meter was added to the side panel too, since that is the readable channel on a phone.
- **Storage guard.** A stored best of `Infinity` or `1e999` survived the old `Number(read(...)) || 0`; the read is now finite-checked.
- **Auto-start audit: clean.** Eleven seconds on the menu with no input leaves the mode and the track clock untouched.
- **Two probe bugs worth recording, not game bugs.** My first run reported 42 idle frames and zero recipe hits. The frames were the opening banner legitimately animating itself out — the measurement was taken before it cleared. The recipe failure was my own loose re-implementation using a stale event snapshot; rewritten to mirror the real recipe in `scripts/capture-games.mjs` step for step, including its `advanceTime` cadence, it passes. The real capture harness had already run the real recipe green throughout.
- Verified: a 21-assertion Playwright probe — the menu costs zero frames once the banner clears and four seconds of real time changes nothing; **the track clock runs at the same tempo at 60Hz and 120Hz while the 120Hz run genuinely renders twice the frames, and two seconds of wall-clock really is about two seconds of track**; all three sync verdicts match the scorer exactly; the drawn band matches the rule and is confirmed lit by canvas pixel sampling; `advanceTime` is byte-identical across two fresh loads; the real recipe still lands hits; a cleared-and-reloaded blind-syncing run fails with its score banked and saved; the phone keeps its primary action above the fold, shows the sync verdict at full size, and has no horizontal overflow; 20 corrupt-storage seeds leave a finite best with zero page errors.
