## 2026-08-13 Claude — overhaul campaign batch 5a: Signal Loom shows the beat you are waiting for

First landing of batch 5. One real bug and one readability rebuild, plus a finding left alone — and this game needed unusual care, because the gate that exists specifically to protect it forbids the obvious version of the fix.

- **The loop never stopped.** `requestAnimationFrame(realtimeLoop)` was re-queued unconditionally, so the page ran an animation frame forever — sitting on the menu before pressing Start, and after the run had already ended. Measured before the fix: 43 frames per 700ms on the menu and 42 after game over. It now sleeps outside play and wakes on Start, on a new stage, and on visibility change; the same measurements are now 0 and 0.
- **The trap in that fix, and why it needed care.** `scripts/check-realtime-progression.mjs` exists *because of this game*: it once shipped with a clock that only ticked inside `advanceTime`, which made stages 2 and up unwinnable for real players while CI stayed green. That gate drives Signal Loom through real elapsed time with no `advanceTime` call and asserts the gate beat climbs. So the sleep condition could not be "is something animating" — it has to stay alive for the whole of `playing`. It does, and the gate was run in full to prove it, alongside the full `check-best-on-fail-persistence` gate which also lists this game.
- **The readability gap: the whole game is timing, and the timing was nearly invisible.** The gate opens on one beat in every four to six, each beat is 560ms, and pulsing on a shut gate costs double. All the board showed was a gate tile flipping between red and yellow and a line of text reading `Beat 0/3 - gate opens on 1`. You could not see the open beat coming, only notice it had arrived. There is now a beat timeline across the bottom: every slot in the cycle, the open one marked and gold, and a moving head showing exactly where in the current beat you are, with a plain `GATE IN 2 BEATS` countdown beside it.
- **Show the consequence before you commit.** A bar above the board states what pulsing right now would do — either `Pulse reaches the core and opens Tension Cross (+96)` or `Pulse now: route unaligned, gate shut - costs 2 charge (6 left), 5 score`. It comes from a new pure `pulseOutcome()` that `pulse()` itself executes from, so the two cannot disagree. The double cost of a shut-gate pulse was a hidden rule and is now stated before you pay it. The side panel echoes it as `Pulse costs 2` and `Wait 2 beats`, which is what stays readable on a phone.
- **Storage guard.** A stored best of `Infinity` or `1e999` survived the old `Number(read(...)) || 0`; the read is now finite-checked.
- **Finding, not fixed: score can be farmed by rotating.** Each conduit rotation awards 1 point with no cap, so holding the rotate key inflates the score and makes Best meaningless. Exactly as with Bulb Brigade, `check-best-on-fail-persistence.mjs` drives this game by pressing Space 60 times to bank score and then pulsing until the charge dies, and it requires a score above zero at that failure — so capping rotation score low enough to matter would break that shared contract. Fixing it properly means changing the gate's driver too, which deserves a deliberate decision rather than a drive-by edit.
- Verified: a 23-assertion Playwright probe — the menu costs zero animation frames and four seconds of real time changes nothing; the gate beat advances under real time during play and the loop stays alive throughout; the pulse preview matches what pulsing does in all three situations (unaligned, aligned with a shut gate, aligned on the open beat); the countdown reads zero exactly when the gate is open and genuinely counts down; a full run aligning each route and waiting for the open beat clears all four stages and saves its score; a cleared-and-reloaded losing run still banks one; **the open beat slot is confirmed gold on the timeline by sampling the canvas pixels**; `advanceTime` is byte-identical across two fresh loads; the phone keeps its primary action above the fold with no horizontal overflow; 20 corrupt-storage seeds leave a finite best with zero page errors. Plus the **full `check-realtime-progression` gate** and the **full `check-best-on-fail-persistence` gate (14 games)**, scoped `test:games`, `capture:games` (desktop and mobile both 0), `audit:contrast` clean, the other static gates, `npm test` 56/56, and `git diff --check` clean. Protected meta, JSON-LD, CSP and runtime blocks verified byte-identical to HEAD.

## 2026-08-13 Claude — overhaul campaign batch 4f: Penalty Circuit ran at double speed and drew a shot it never took

Sixth and final landing of batch 4. Two real bugs and one hidden rule made visible, plus a design finding I chose not to act on — stated plainly, including a rebalance I tried, simulated, and abandoned because it did not work.

- **Double speed on a 120Hz display.** The live loop called `stepMs(1000/60)` once per animation frame regardless of how much time had actually passed, so at 120Hz the ball flew twice as fast, the keeper closed twice as fast, and the 1.35 second flight timeout expired in 0.675 seconds of real time. In a game that is entirely about timing and judgement, that is a material difference. The loop now consumes real elapsed time in fixed 1/60 slices through its own accumulator, so a slice is only spent when the wall clock has earned it. `advanceTime` keeps calling the untouched fixed-step `stepMs`, so both harnesses stay deterministic. The probe proves the fix by driving the game's own loop from a synthetic clock at 60Hz and at 120Hz and confirming the ball reaches the same place after the same 400ms of simulated wall-clock, with the 120Hz run genuinely rendering twice the frames.
- **The aim preview drew a shot the ball never took.** The dashed path was a hand-drawn quadratic with a control point offset by `curve * 90`, and its target circle sat at a fixed height ignoring curve entirely. The real flight drifts up to about 60px sideways, so the marker was wrong by up to 12% of the goal width and the drawn bow was roughly double the real one. The preview is now the actual flight: launch, spin, step and stop conditions were extracted into shared functions that both `shoot()` and the preview run through, and the dashed line is the simulated path with the marker at the true landing point. The probe checks five different aim, power and curve settings and requires the predicted landing point to match the flown one within 1.5px.
- **The keeper's read was a hidden rule that punished sensible play.** The keeper reads the shot by `0.24 + |aim|*0.16 + power*0.22 - |curve|*0.32`, so more power makes him read you better and more curve makes him read you worse. Nothing anywhere said this, and "more power is good" is what most players will assume. There is now a keeper-read bar on the pitch and a Read pill in the status row, both computed from the same `readOf()` the shot itself uses, with a plain line stating that power raises it and curve lowers it. His dive *direction* is deliberately still not shown — the strength is the rule worth teaching, the direction is the part still worth guessing.
- **Storage guard.** A stored best of `Infinity` or `1e999` survived the old `Number(...) || 0`; the read is now finite-checked.
- **A rebalance I tried and abandoned, honestly.** Brute-forcing all 28,577 combinations of aim, power and curve shows the game is fully deterministic — no randomness anywhere — and that 50.1% of shots score. So once a player finds one scoring shot they can repeat it five times for a guaranteed 5/5, and there is never a reason to vary. I prototyped a keeper who also commits toward where you have been shooting, simulated a five-shot round for both a repeating player and an adapting one, and the repeater still scored 5/5 because far-corner shots beat the keeper's clamped reach regardless. Since the change did not achieve its purpose, I reverted it rather than shipping a balance edit that only looks like a fix. **The repeatability remains an open finding**: a real fix needs the keeper's reach or the goal geometry reconsidered, which deserves a deliberate decision rather than a drive-by change.
- **Phone layout.** Adding the Read pill initially left the Shoot button three pixels above the capture harness's buried-action threshold, which is far too fragile. Phones now lay the status row out in three columns instead of two, which shrank it from 195px to 131px and moved Shoot up to y=524 against a 591 threshold — higher than it sat before this pass, with the extra readout included.
- Verified: a 21-assertion Playwright probe — the pitch waits with zero animation frames and four seconds of real time changes nothing; the drawn preview lands where the ball lands across five settings and curve genuinely bends the flight; the read bar equals the read the shot uses, rises with power and falls with curve; the 60Hz and 120Hz synthetic-clock runs agree; `advanceTime` is byte-identical across two fresh loads; a full five-shot round reaches game over and saves its result; **the read bar is confirmed to redraw by sampling the canvas pixels along it**; the phone keeps Shoot above the fold, takes a shot, and has no horizontal overflow; 20 corrupt-storage seeds leave a finite best with zero page errors. Plus scoped `test:games`, `capture:games` (desktop and mobile both 0), `audit:contrast` clean, all the static gates, `npm test` 56/56, and `git diff --check` clean. Protected meta, JSON-LD, CSP and runtime blocks verified byte-identical to HEAD.

**Batch 4 is complete**: canopy-courier, gridfront-orders, ledger-lanes, diamond-derby, bulb-brigade, penalty-circuit. Twenty-eight of the hundred games have now been rebuilt or repaired.

## 2026-08-13 Claude — overhaul campaign batch 4e: Bulb Brigade puts the whole pattern on the board

Fifth landing of batch 4. One real bug, one readability rebuild of the board, and two findings I deliberately did not act on — all stated plainly.

- **The real defect: nothing decayed outside `advanceTime`.** The page has no animation loop at all, so `state.flash` (the pulse ring, nominally 420ms) and the status banner timer only ever shrank inside `window.advanceTime`. In real play the pulse ring was drawn at its initial radius forever. Measured before the fix: the ring pixel at the core was byte-identical two seconds after a pulse, and the page ran 0 animation frames. This is the same class of defect as Gridfront Orders, and again invisible to every gate because the gates drive the game through `advanceTime`. There is now a loop that runs **only while the ring or the banner is still fading**, steps by real elapsed time clamped at 100ms, sleeps as soon as both reach zero, and wakes on visibility change. The fixed-step `advanceTime` path is untouched and stays byte-identical across two fresh loads.
- **The readability gap: the board did not show the puzzle.** The game asks you to cycle each lens slot to an authored target, but the canvas only ever drew the *current* lens. The one place the target appeared was a side-panel line naming a single slot at a time. The board now labels every slot with the lens it needs and how many cycles away it is, marks solved slots `SET`, and draws each slot's line to the core green when it matches and red when it does not — so the whole pattern is legible at a glance instead of one instruction at a time.
- **Show the consequence before you commit.** Pulsing with a wrong pattern silently costs a charge and score. A bar under the board now states exactly what pulsing will do right now — either `Pulse splits wave 2 of 3 (+54)` or `Pulse now: 3 lenses are wrong, costs 1 charge (3 left) and 12 score`. It comes from a new pure `pulseOutcome()` that `pulse()` itself executes from, so the promise and the result are the same code. The probe checks this at every wrong-count from 0 to 4.
- **Storage guard.** A stored best of `Infinity` or `1e999` survived the old `Number(read(...)) || 0`; the read is now finite-checked.
- **Finding, not fixed: score can be farmed without limit.** Each lens cycle awards 2 points with no cap, so holding Space indefinitely inflates the score and makes Best meaningless. I left it alone deliberately: `check-best-on-fail-persistence.mjs` drives this game by pressing Space 120 times and then pulsing until the core dies, and it requires a score above zero at that failure. Capping cycle score low enough to matter would take the banked score below the wrong-pulse drain and break that shared contract. Fixing it properly means changing the gate's driver too, which is a decision worth making on purpose rather than in passing.
- **Finding, not fixed: the game has no real decision.** The side panel names the exact next action, and there is no other way to learn the authored pattern, so play is "read the instruction, obey, repeat". Putting the pattern on the board makes it readable and lets you plan the cheapest order, but it does not create a choice. Turning this into a genuine deduction or optimisation game would be a redesign, which the frozen-catalog policy rules out without an explicit instruction.
- **Noted: the phone canvas is small.** The board renders into a 960-wide store scaled onto a 376-wide phone, so the new slot labels are legible but tiny. Unlike Diamond Derby, this game stays fully playable there because the HTML side panel already carries every control and readout at full size, so a compact store here is polish rather than a playability fix and was not attempted in this pass.
- Verified: a 22-assertion Playwright probe — the menu costs zero animation frames and four seconds of real time changes nothing; matched and unmatched slots are confirmed green and red **by sampling the canvas pixels on each line**; the pulse preview equals the real pulse at every wrong-count; a clean run clears all four stages and saves its score; a cleared-and-reloaded failing run still banks a losing score; the loop animates a pulse under real time and sleeps again once it fades; `advanceTime` is byte-identical across fresh loads; the capture recipe still reaches a successful pulse; the phone keeps its primary action above the fold with taps still selecting slots and no horizontal overflow; 20 corrupt-storage seeds leave a finite best with zero page errors. Plus the **full `check-best-on-fail-persistence` gate (all 14 games)** and the **full `check-reduced-motion` gate (35 motion-aware games)**, both of which list this game, scoped `test:games`, `capture:games` (desktop and mobile both 0), `audit:contrast` clean, the other static gates, `npm test` 56/56, and `git diff --check` clean. Protected meta, JSON-LD, CSP and runtime blocks verified byte-identical to HEAD.

## 2026-08-13 Claude — overhaul campaign batch 4d: Diamond Derby played itself and lost before you touched it

Fourth landing of batch 4, and the worst real defect the campaign has found so far. Two genuine bugs plus a readability fix, not a rewrite.

- **The game started without the player and could finish without them.** The pitch clock ran from page load. A probe that loads the page, waits, and reads the diagnostics with no input at all shows `phase: "pitch"` at load and `phase: "fail", pitch: 5/5, homers: 0` about thirteen seconds later. Anyone who read the instructions first lost round one having never swung. The capture recipe even carried a comment working around it: it presses `n` first because "real-time idle play before this evaluate can otherwise park the game in a settled fail state". There is now a `ready` phase: the ballpark waits, shows a `PLAY BALL` prompt, and the first Swing, Space, Enter or tap faces the first pitch. `n` still starts a live derby exactly as before, so the capture recipe is untouched and still reaches its settled home run.
- **The animation loop never stopped.** `requestAnimationFrame` was re-queued unconditionally, so a finished derby kept redrawing forever — measured at 42 frames per 700ms sitting on the game-over screen. The loop now sleeps outside live play and wakes on the next pitch or on visibility change; the same measurement is now 0 frames while waiting and 0 once the derby is settled.
- **You could not know what your swing needed to carry.** Carry is `340 x power x angle`, so perfect contact at the middle angle goes 442 ft and solid contact goes 374 ft, against fences of 370, 388, 401, 416 and 432 ft. From round two onward solid contact is worthless, and from round four only the middle angle can clear at all — none of which was visible. The board now shows, at the current angle, what perfect and solid contact carry and whether each clears this round's fence (`Good 374 ft — short by 14`), with matching markers on the field. It reads from the same `carry()` used by the real swing, so it cannot drift.
- **Confirmed the derby is winnable before touching balance.** The probe plays a perfect-timing run through all five rounds and reaches `win`, so the escalating fences are hard but fair and no rebalancing was warranted.
- **Phone layout.** The ballpark rendered into a 960-wide store scaled onto a 376-wide phone, making the timing bar about seven pixels tall with a marker barely one pixel wide — the core mechanic was close to unreadable. The compact store is 560x460 and the ballpark is repositioned into it while every element keeps its size in store pixels, which renders the bat, ball, timing bar and carry readout about 1.7x larger. The phone HUD went to three columns and the touch row to four, so both the primary button and the Swing button ended up higher on the page than before, not lower.
- **Smaller fixes.** A stored `longest` of `Infinity` or `1e999` survived the old `Number(v.longest) || 0` read; stats are now finite-checked and `bestRound` is clamped to the five real rounds. Space was a dead key on the game-over and champion screens and now retries the round or starts a new derby.
- Verified: a 22-assertion Playwright probe — waits at ready through six seconds of real time with zero animation frames; Swing starts play and the loop runs; **the advertised carry equals the distance a real perfect swing produces across all 15 round-and-angle combinations, including whether it clears**; a perfect-timing run wins all five rounds and saves the champion result; Space works on both terminal screens; the loop sleeps once settled; `advanceTime` is byte-identical across two fresh loads; the phone store is 560 wide with both buttons above the fold and no horizontal overflow; 10 corrupt-storage seeds leave finite stats with zero page errors. A separate **canvas pixel probe** confirms the field markers are drawn red when short and green when clearing, sampled at round two. Plus scoped `test:games`, `capture:games` (desktop and mobile both 0), `audit:contrast` clean, the **full `check-reduced-motion` gate** (this game is on its list), the other static gates, `test:best-score-guard`, `npm test` 56/56, and `git diff --check` clean. Protected meta, JSON-LD, CSP and runtime blocks verified byte-identical to HEAD.

## 2026-08-13 Claude — overhaul campaign batch 4c: Ledger Lanes shows the money before you commit

Third landing of batch 4. Mostly a readability rebuild of the board, plus one small real timing bug — said plainly, because the timing bug is the minor half here.

- **The real defect, and its honest size.** The animation loop called `update(1/60)` once per animation frame regardless of elapsed time. The only time-driven state in this game is the status banner's dwell timer, so the effect was that the banner vanished in half the intended time on a 120Hz display. Real, but modest. The loop now steps by real elapsed time clamped at 100ms, skips work while the tab is hidden, and wakes on visibility change. The fixed-step `advanceTime` path is untouched, and a probe confirms two fresh loads produce byte-identical diagnostics after the same simulated sequence.
- **The readability gap, which was the whole game.** Settlement pays `sold x price`, plus 18 for an exact fill, minus 5 per unit over, 3 per unit short, and 4 per point of risk, and the player pays each card's cost up front plus an 8 fee for a mismatch. None of that arithmetic was visible anywhere. You drafted cards, pressed Settle, and found out afterwards. The canvas meanwhile duplicated the HTML side panel almost field for field, so it carried no information the panel did not already have.
- **The board now carries the arithmetic instead.** Each lane shows its fill against demand as a colour-coded bar (amber short, green exact, red over) and then the actual line items — `Sell 4 at 15`, `Exact fill +18`, `Risk 2 -8` — ending in what that lane pays right now. A headline gives the whole book: settle this instant and bank this much. Each card in hand is priced into the currently selected lane, so you can compare all four at a glance before spending one. The same per-lane and per-card figures were added to the HTML side panel, which is the control surface on a phone.
- **The forecast cannot drift, by construction.** Settlement was extracted into one pure `settleLanes()` routine. The real settle, the board headline, the per-lane figures and the per-card forecast all run through it, and the card forecast is simply the difference between settling with and without that card, minus its cost and any mismatch fee. The probe checks this exhaustively: **all 60 card-into-lane combinations across all five days**, asserting the advertised number equals the real change in banked profit plus what the bell would pay. It also asserts the settle-now headline equals the profit each settle actually delivers.
- **Confirmed the target is fair before touching balance.** A brute force over all 4^4 assignments per day (offline, not shipped) puts the best possible book at 601 against a target of 260, while the average over all plays is 36 and simple colour-matching scores 483. So the target rewards thinking without being unreachable, and no rebalancing was warranted. The probe plays a winning line end to end and confirms `won`, and a run that drafts nothing ends `failed` without overwriting a better saved best.
- **Smaller fixes.** A stored best of `Infinity` or `1e999` used to survive the old `Number(...) || 0` read and display as Infinity; the read is now finite-checked. On a phone the 900-wide board shrank to illegible specks, so the compact store is 440x560 and shows the selected lane's arithmetic at full size rather than three unreadable columns. The board panel no longer stretches to match the side panel, which removed an empty box under the canvas.
- Verified: a 24-assertion Playwright probe covering all of the above, plus arrow-key and Enter/Space control, the exact capture-recipe sequence, idle/active/sleep animation-frame behaviour, phone store width and tap selection with no horizontal overflow, and 24 corrupt-storage seeds with zero page errors. Plus scoped `test:games`, `capture:games` (desktop and mobile both 0), `audit:contrast` (0 below threshold), all seven static gates, `test:best-score-guard`, `npm test` 56/56, and `git diff --check` clean. Protected meta, JSON-LD, CSP and runtime blocks verified byte-identical to HEAD.

## 2026-08-13 Claude — overhaul campaign batch 4b: Gridfront Orders shows the consequence before you commit

Second landing of batch 4. Part real-bug fix, part readability rebuild — both, honestly, and the readability half is the larger change.

- **The real defect.** The page had no animation loop at all: `tick()` was only ever called by `advanceTime`. So the floating labels the game spawns on every move, hit and kill — `life: 44` frames — never decayed during actual play. They piled up on the board and stayed there until the mission restarted. Every gate passed while this was broken, because every gate drives the game through `advanceTime`, where the labels do fade. There is now a loop that runs **only while a label is still fading**, steps by real elapsed time clamped at 100ms (a fixed step per frame would fade twice as fast on a 120Hz display), sleeps the moment the last label expires, and wakes on visibility change. The fixed-step `advanceTime` path is untouched.
- **The readability gap, which was the bigger problem.** This is a tactics game whose entire rule set — move up to 3, attack range 2, cover halves straight-line fire, hostiles close one square and then shoot at range 2 — was stated in one line of small text above the board and shown nowhere on it. You could not see which squares you could reach, which hostiles you could hit, how much damage you would deal, or whether the square you were about to stand on would get you shot. The board now shows all four: dashed teal outlines on every reachable square, a red wash over every square inside hostile firing range, a dashed targeting ring and exact damage figure (flagged `KO` when lethal) on every hostile in range, and a red `-N` on each reachable square giving the damage the squad takes if the turn ends there.
- **The damage forecast is simulated, not estimated.** The hostile turn was extracted into one pure routine, `enemyPlan()`, and both the real turn and the per-square forecast run through it. The `-N` on a square is literally the output of the same code that will execute — the probe proves the advertised number equals the actual squad health lost, and that the attack preview damage equals the damage dealt.
- **One order line, one source of truth.** `previewOrder()` describes what Order will do and `order()` executes from that description, so the colour-coded line above the board (teal move, red attack, grey blocked) cannot disagree with what pressing Enter does. The cursor box takes the same colour.
- **A keyboard trap, fixed.** The page called `preventDefault()` on every Tab keypress, so a keyboard-only player could never reach the buttons beneath the board. Tab is now intercepted only while the grid itself holds focus, Shift+Tab always escapes, and `Q` switches unit from anywhere. Letters are not text input in this game, so a bare letter key is safe here.
- **Phone layout.** The board used to render into a 960-wide store scaled down to a 376-wide phone, making every token and label a speck. The compact store is 520x520, which nearly doubles the on-screen board. The first attempt at 520x560 pushed the Order button to y=700 and the capture harness correctly flagged it as buried; the store was shortened and the phone HUD tightened until the button sits at y=555 against a 590 threshold.
- Verified: a 23-assertion Playwright probe — the reachable-square count matches an independent recount of the movement rule; a previewed move lands exactly where it said; the advertised damage equals the damage taken; the attack preview equals the damage dealt and correctly flags lethal hits; a full scripted run reaches victory or loss with a banked, persisted score; `advanceTime` is byte-identical across two fresh loads; the idle board never spins a frame, a fresh order does animate under real time, and the loop sleeps again once the label fades; Tab moves focus normally while still switching units on the grid; the phone store is 520 wide and touch buttons still issue orders; 20 corrupt-storage seeds survive with zero page errors. A separate **canvas pixel probe** samples every tile centre and confirms every in-fire square is measurably redder (44 or more) than every clear square (13 or less), so the danger wash matches the fire zone exactly rather than approximately. Plus scoped `test:games`, `capture:games` (desktop and mobile both 0), `audit:contrast` (64 elements, 0 below threshold), all seven static gates, `test:best-score-guard`, `npm test` 56/56, and `git diff --check` clean. Protected meta, JSON-LD, CSP and runtime blocks verified byte-identical to HEAD.

Sixth and final landing of batch 3, and a small, precisely-scoped fix rather than a rewrite — stated plainly because that is what it is.

## 2026-08-13 Claude — overhaul campaign batch 3e: Letter Foundry stops rejecting real words

Fifth landing of batch 3. The capture recipe types `s`, `t`, `o`, `n`, `e` and forges STONE, so round one keeps the STONEAR rack and STONE stays a valid forge. Letters are gameplay input here, so no action is bound to a bare letter key.

- **Same flaw Rune Roster had, and the same fix.** Each rack accepted exactly six words, so a player who typed NOTES or STORE from the STONEAR rack was told they were wrong for not guessing which six the author picked. The lists are now 26 to 82 words per rack, and a load-time filter drops any entry that is under three letters or not actually spellable from its own rack, so a typo in a list can never present a target the letters cannot reach.
- **Rounds now clear at a goal, not by exhaustion.** Clearing used to require finding *every* word on the list, which was fine at six and would have been a slog at eighty. Each round now carries a goal of four or five words and clears when you reach it, with the rest of the list still there to find if you want the extra score. The progress bar tracks the goal rather than the whole list, and the header reads "Forge 5 words" instead of "Forge all words".
- **The found panel stopped leaking the answers.** It used to render one blanked chip per word, which both spelled out exactly how many letters every unfound word had and would have become a wall of eighty blanks. It now shows what you have forged, a count against the goal, and how many more words the rack still knows.
- Verified: a 7-assertion Playwright probe — the exact capture-recipe sequence still forges STONE; a word the old six-word list rejected is now accepted and scores; reaching the goal clears the round; 16 corrupt-storage seeds across the game's own keys survive with zero page errors. Plus scoped `test:games`, `capture:games` (desktop and mobile both 0, screenshot reviewed and the new counters confirmed), `audit:contrast` (92 elements, 0 below threshold), all seven static gates, `npm test` 56/56, and `git diff --check` clean. ## 2026-08-13 Claude — overhaul campaign batch 3d: Beacon Bastion shows the pulse range and the shades you cannot see

Fourth landing of batch 3, and like Aster Vault a targeted pass rather than a rewrite — this game is also pinned by two harnesses (its capture recipe drives `#wardBtn` and `#pulseBtn`, and `driveBeaconBastion` in `check-best-on-fail-persistence.mjs` pulses to bank score then stops defending, expecting mode `fail` — note this game uses `fail`, not `failed` — with the score written to `beacon-bastion.best.v1`).

**Checked first, found clean:** unlike Aster Vault, this game's loop was already correct — it steps by real elapsed time clamped at 50ms and already gates itself on `state.mode === "playing"`, so neither the double-speed nor the never-sleeping defect existed here. Worth recording that the audit found nothing rather than implying every real-time game shared the fault.

Two genuine fairness problems did exist, and both are fixed:

- **You could not see the pulse range.** Pulse damages shades within 220 pixels of the beacon, but nothing drew that boundary, so firing a five-second cooldown was a guess about whether anything was even in reach. The radius is now drawn as a dashed ring — gold and labelled "PULSE READY — E" when it is off cooldown, grey and counting down the remaining seconds when it is not.
- **Shades spawned off the visible board.** Every level's spawn points sit outside the canvas (x of −30 or 990, y of −30 or 570), so threats walked in from places the player physically could not look at. Incoming shades that are off-screen now show a red arrow pinned to the nearest edge, pointing the way they are travelling, so an attack from behind is something you can react to rather than something that simply happens to you.

## 2026-08-13 Claude — overhaul campaign batch 3c: Aster Vault gets a flight forecast, and two real bugs fixed

Third landing of batch 3. **Scope note, stated plainly:** unlike the previous eight games this was a targeted deepening pass, not a ground-up rewrite. Aster Vault is a real-time physics game pinned by *two* harnesses at once — `asterVaultRecipe()` in the capture script and `driveAsterVault` in `check-best-on-fail-persistence.mjs`, which both fire real thrust and brake input through the exact physics constants and read `ship.speed`, `relics[].taken` and `recommended.thrustX/thrustY`. Rewriting that physics wholesale mid-session was the wrong risk, so the flight model is untouched and the work went into two genuine defects and the readability of the game.

- **Bug one: the game ran at double speed on a fast monitor.** The loop called `update(1/60)` on every animation frame regardless of how much time had actually passed, so on a 120Hz display everything — drift, oxygen burn, the whole run — went twice as fast as designed. It now steps by real elapsed time, clamped at 50ms so a background tab cannot teleport the ship. The fixed-step path used by `advanceTime` is deliberately unchanged, so both harnesses stay deterministic; the probe confirms two fresh loads advance to an identical oxygen reading.
- **Bug two: the animation loop never stopped.** `ensureLoop` started it and nothing ever cancelled it, so the page burned frames forever — on the menu, after a win, after a breach. It now sleeps whenever the run is idle and nothing is animating, and wakes on input. The probe measures the idle menu and sees the loop asleep.
- **A flight forecast is the readability win.** The ship now projects its own path forward under current velocity, drawn as a dashed line, and if that path ends in a rift the line turns red and rings the impact point. An inertial game where you cannot see where inertia is taking you is guesswork; this makes the consequence visible before you commit, the same idea as Seedline's sow preview and Flux Reversi's flip preview.
- **Feel and sound.** Thrusters now trail sparks, recovering a relic bursts violet particles and rings a chime that climbs with each relic aboard, a rift brush and a suit breach shake the frame, and a low-oxygen warning pulses a red border with a plain "OXYGEN LOW" label. The single-oscillator beep is replaced by a layered synth with separate voices for launch, relics, braking hiss, rift contact, stage clear, victory and breach. All decorative motion is gated on `prefers-reduced-motion`, which the diagnostics now report.
- Verified: a 12-assertion Playwright probe — the idle menu sleeps; `advanceTime` is deterministic across loads; `recommended`, `relics[].taken` and `ship.speed` all still report as the harnesses expect; thrusting on the recommendation really does reach a relic; braking the oxygen away fails the run with the banked score persisted; 16 corrupt-storage seeds across both keys survive with zero page errors. Plus **the real `check-best-on-fail-persistence` gate run in full (all 14 games pass)**, scoped `test:games`, `capture:games` (desktop and mobile both 0, screenshot reviewed), `audit:contrast` (82 elements, 0 below threshold), all seven static gates, `npm test` 56/56, and `git diff --check` clean. Protected meta/JSON-LD blocks verified byte-identical to HEAD.

## 2026-08-13 Claude — overhaul campaign batch 3b: Chromalock counts the codes that still fit

Second landing of batch 3, still inline. The capture recipe types two four-peg guesses on the opening board, so the first lock stays four pegs over ten guesses and digits 1-6 still place colours with Enter submitting.

- **The deduction is now visible.** After every clue the game filters all possible codes against every clue given so far and prints how many still fit — 1296 at the start of a four-peg lock, often a dozen after two guesses, single digits near the end. That one number turns Mastermind from guesswork into deduction you can watch narrowing, and it costs nothing: at most 6^5 = 7776 codes to check. The probe recomputes the same filter independently on every guess of a full run and asserts the game's count matches exactly.
- **A vault ladder replaces the single code.** A run is now five locks — Brass and Copper at four pegs over ten then nine guesses, then Silver, Cobalt and Onyx at five pegs over ten, nine and eight. Cracking a lock scores 120 plus 30 per guess left plus 60 for cracking it without a hint, the run total carries across locks, and failing any lock ends the run. Best run score, best crack (fewest guesses) and streak all persist.
- **A hint** loads a code that still fits every clue given so far — never a random guess, and never the answer handed over — and forfeits that lock's clean-crack bonus. The probe verifies the hinted code really is consistent with every clue on the board.
- **Feel.** Pegs pop in as you place them with a pitch that rises across the palette, clue markers pop in one at a time over a rising tick, cracking a lock bursts particles and shakes the board, and the synth gained separate voices for placing, submitting, clues, a lock opening, the vault opening, and a lockout. On desktop the right column's best-run line had been printing straight through the palette label — spotted in the screenshot, not by a gate — and is now spaced properly.

## 2026-08-13 Claude — overhaul campaign batch 3a: Seedline shows you the sow before you commit

First landing of batch 3, still inline. The capture recipe presses `1` and lets the CPU reply, so digit keys still sow pits 1-6 directly and the CPU still answers inside `advanceTime(900)`.

- **The sow preview is the headline.** Counting the loop is the entire skill of a mancala, and the old build made you do it in your head against a board of bare numbers. Selecting a pit now rings every pit that sow would drop a seed into, badges each with how many it receives, rings a capture target in gold, and prints one plain line underneath — "This sow captures 7 seeds", "This sow ends in your store — extra turn", or the neutral prompt. The rules stop being hidden arithmetic.
- **Seeds now actually travel.** Each sown seed arcs from the picked pit to its destination on a short stagger, over a rising pentatonic tick, so a twelve-seed sow reads as a run around the board. Captures burst particles and shake the board, banking into a store pulses the count, and the synth gained distinct voices for store, capture, extra turn, and the three end states. State updates immediately and the animation is purely cosmetic, so diagnostics stay truthful mid-flight.
- **Three CPU levels** behind buttons and the `C` key: Casual searches one ply and wanders a third of the time, though it still takes a free extra turn or capture when one is sitting there; Standard searches four; Expert searches eight and buys three to five extra plies once the board thins below twenty and twelve seeds, because the endgame is where a mancala is won. The evaluation now weighs store difference, seeds held on each side, and how close each seed sits to its own store. A hint (button or `H`) runs the same search from your side at expert depth and rings the pit.
- **Honest measurement:** a bot that follows the game's own expert-depth hints and moves first still beats every level — 41-7 against Casual in 7 sows, 34-14 against Standard in 16, and 35-13 against Expert in 22. Kalah's first player has a real advantage and this evaluation is a heuristic, not a solver, so Expert is best described as "makes you work three times as long", not "hard to beat". The levels are genuinely ordered; the top one is not unbeatable.
- Mobile switches to a narrower 620 by 470 backing store with an enlarged score header, and the six large numbered pit buttons that were already in the markup carry touch input. The board slab also now actually contains the two stores, which had been hanging off its bottom edge since before this pass.

## 2026-08-13 Claude — overhaul campaign batch 2f: Blackjack gets the missing half of the rules

Sixth inline landing, and the last of batch 2. The capture recipe presses `s`, `Enter`, `h`, `s` with no start button, so the game still boots into a betting state where stand is a no-op, Enter deals, and hit and stand play the hand. Play-chips only, unchanged.

- **Split was simply missing.** The game is now a real multi-hand blackjack: any two cards of equal value can be split, up to four hands, each carrying its own wager, with the standard restrictions — split aces take exactly one card each and stand, and a split hand reaching 21 is **not** a natural and pays even money rather than 3 to 2.
- **The dealer now peeks.** When the up-card is an ace or a ten-value card the hole is checked before you act, so you can no longer double or split into a dealer blackjack that was already sitting there.
- **The rules are stated on screen** rather than left implicit: the dealer stands on all 17s, blackjack pays 3 to 2, split hands that reach 21 pay even money. The table itself prints the first two.
- **Session arc.** Bankroll depletion now ends the session with a real summary — hands played, wins, pushes, peak chips, best streak, blackjacks — instead of quietly refusing to deal, and New Game rebuys. Peak chips and best streak persist.
- **Feel.** Cards now fly in from the shoe with an eased slide, the hole card flips through a squash, chips arc from your stack to the pot on a bet or a double and back on a payout, naturals and wins throw particles, busts shake the table, and the synth is layered — a noise-backed card slide, a two-tone chip clink, a three-note win chord, a five-note blackjack fanfare, and a noise-and-saw bust. All gated on `prefers-reduced-motion`, audio suspends on tab hide, and the loop sleeps when nothing moves.
- **Payouts are verified against a hand-checked table, not eyeballed.** The probe replays dozens of real hands with a strategy that deliberately splits and doubles, then recomputes every settled hand's payout independently — bust 0, natural 2.5x stake back, dealer natural 0, both natural push, dealer bust 2x, higher total 2x, lower 0, equal push — and asserts the game matches, that the chip balance equals stake out plus payout in, that no split hand ever claims a natural, and that the dealer never stands under 17 in a round that actually reached its turn. **A first run flagged the dealer standing on 14; that turned out to be my assertion, not the game** — a player natural settles the round immediately and the dealer correctly stops drawing, so the check now excludes rounds settled early.
- **Mobile needed a different fix from the last three games.** Making the canvas taller does nothing here, because the backing-store *width* sets the scale on a phone. The canvas now switches to a narrower 600 by 520 store below ~560 CSS pixels, which roughly doubles the apparent size of the cards and text. A first attempt at 600 by 820 was caught by the capture harness as "primary action buried" — the taller board pushed the Deal button below the fold — so the height came back down.
- Verified: a 17-assertion Playwright probe (payout audit above, the exact capture-recipe sequence, a split actually occurring, a natural paid 3:2, bankroll depletion ending the session, New Game rebuying, bet controls, 16 corrupt-storage seeds, idle rAF asleep, no page errors), scoped `test:games`, `capture:games` (desktop and mobile both 0, screenshots reviewed at both sizes), `audit:contrast` (40 elements, 0 below threshold), all seven static gates, `npm test` 56/56, and `git diff --check` clean. ## 2026-08-13 Claude — overhaul campaign batch 2e: Shard Sheriff stops printing the answer

Fifth inline landing. Like Rune Roster, this game is pinned by two harnesses at once: `shardSheriffRecipe()` in `scripts/capture-games.mjs` starts the game, steers `aim` toward `recommended.angle` with arrow keys until it is within one degree, then fires and expects a hit; and `driveShardSheriff` in `scripts/check-best-on-fail-persistence.mjs` fires stage 0 at aim 0 to clear it, then steers stage 1 (which loads at aim −25) back toward 0 and fires into the glass at (530,278) to fail with the banked score written to `shard-sheriff.best.v2`. Both still pass unchanged, so the board geometry is now documented as a hard contract at the top of the file: shooter at (96,280), play rectangle 42..878 by 48..512, glass radius 24, target radius 25.

- **The design problem was that the game told you the answer.** The side panel printed "Recommended: −24°" and you typed that number in with the arrow keys. The recommendation still exists in `render_game_to_text` because the capture recipe depends on it, but the panel no longer shows it. In its place the barrel now draws a **dashed sight line out to its first bank**, so you aim by reading the geometry, and each badge lists only how many banks its intended solution takes. A new **Long Sight** action (button or `S`) spends one shard to reveal the entire predicted path for the rest of the stage — a real risk-reward call rather than a free answer.
- **Solvability is now proved at load instead of hand-authored.** Every target used to carry a hardcoded "correct" angle. Each target's angle is now solved by scanning the whole aim range in quarter-degree steps and keeping the first angle where that angle *and both neighbours one degree either side* all land the same hit without touching glass. That guarantees every stage is winnable by construction, and it guarantees the capture recipe connects even though it only aligns to within a degree. The probe independently confirms every target in all seven stages resolves.
- **More game.** Three new stages (seven total: Gallows Row, Ricochet Run, Sheriff's Last Stand) and scoring that rewards the clever shot — 40 base plus 25 per wall banked before the hit plus 5 per shard still in the badge, a 50-point bonus for clearing a stage without a miss, a per-stage clear bonus, and an end-of-run rank from S to C based on misses and clean stages.
- **Feel.** The tracer now draws progressively along its path with a bright head, the muzzle flashes, hit badges and broken glass throw particles, the board shakes on a kill or a spoiled warrant, and the synth is layered: a noise-backed firing thump, a ping per ricochet that climbs in pitch with each bank, a three-note chord on a hit, a noise-burst glass shatter, and stage and victory fanfares. Everything decorative is gated on `prefers-reduced-motion`, audio suspends on tab hide, and the loop sleeps whenever nothing is moving. The board is also tappable now — touch anywhere and the barrel points there.
- **Deliberate exception:** this game does *not* get the taller mobile canvas the last three received. Its physics are defined in fixed canvas coordinates that the two harnesses fire real shots through, so changing the play-area geometry would change the game the probes assert on. Mobile instead gets an enlarged in-canvas header showing the stage, aim and shard count, and the small desktop subtitle is dropped.
- Verified: a 20-assertion Playwright probe — every target in all seven stages has a solution angle and aiming at those angles wins the run with a rank and a persisted best; the exact capture-recipe sequence lands a hit and leaves the run alive; the best-on-fail driver still reaches a scored failure with the best written and surviving reload; Long Sight costs exactly one shard and cannot be bought twice; running out of shards ends the run; 16 corrupt-storage seeds across both keys survive with zero page errors; the idle menu does not spin the animation loop. Plus scoped `test:games`, `capture:games` (desktop and mobile both 0, screenshots reviewed), `audit:contrast` (70 elements, 0 below threshold), all seven static gates, `npm test` 56/56, and `git diff --check` clean. ## 2026-08-13 Claude — overhaul campaign batch 2d: Rune Roster stops being a guessing game

Fourth inline landing (see 2a for the strategy change that retired the parallel-subagent approach). This one has unusually tight contracts: `scripts/capture-games.mjs` drives a dedicated `runeRosterRecipe()` that starts the game, types `t-o-n-e`, and submits — expecting a scoring word — and `scripts/check-best-on-fail-persistence.mjs` drives stage 0 by name ("Vowel Lock", rack STONERA, locked rune T, integrity 6), spells "tone", then drains integrity with empty submits and requires a `failed` mode with a positive score written to `rune-roster.best.v2`. Both are preserved exactly.

- **The core fix: the word lists were tiny.** Each stage accepted only eight authored words, so a player typing a perfectly good word like NOTES or STORE was punished for not guessing which eight the author picked. Stage lists are now 15 to 45 words each (the opening rack alone accepts 40, including SENATOR and TREASON, which use all seven runes). The game now rewards vocabulary instead of mind-reading. A load-time filter drops any authored entry that is not at least four runes, does not contain the locked rune, or is not actually spellable from that rack, so a typo in a list can never present an unreachable target — and the probe re-checks all five lists independently.
- **Scoring has shape now.** Words score length squared, multiplied by a combo that climbs 25% per consecutive accepted word up to triple. Clearing a stage without a single mistake banks a 50-point clean bonus and counts toward a final rank (S through C on clean stages), and each stage cleared pays a stage bonus. The end screen reports score, rank, best, longest combo, and clean stages.
- **A hint** spends one integrity to reveal a real word's first two runes and its length, which is a genuine risk-reward call rather than a free answer.
- **Feel.** Picked runes tween inward with a squash, the locked rune carries a steady gold ring, accepted words fire a particle burst with an expanding ring pulse and a floating score, rejects shake the board, and the synth is layered — a rising ladder as you pick runes, a three-note chord on an accepted word that climbs with the combo, stage and victory fanfares, and a two-oscillator reject. All decorative motion is gated on `prefers-reduced-motion`, audio suspends when the tab hides, and the animation loop sleeps whenever nothing is moving. Runes on the canvas are now tappable directly, not just through the side buttons.
- **A real bug caught only by playtesting.** I first bound the hint to a bare `H`, which silently ate the letter H while spelling — and the fourth stage's rack is MORPHEA, so HOME became OME and HOMER became OMER, each rejection costing integrity until the run died. Every automated gate passed while this was broken; only driving a full five-stage run surfaced it. The hint is now `Alt` + `H`, matching the file's existing modifier convention, leaving every letter key free for spelling.
- Mobile switches the canvas to a taller backing store so the rune ring is finger-sized, and the ring radius and word box were retuned at both sizes after the screenshots showed side runes sitting behind the box.
- Verified: a 24-assertion Playwright probe — every authored word in all five stages is spellable and legal; playing each stage's own list wins the run with a rank and a persisted best; the exact capture-recipe sequence still scores TONE and leaves the stage running; the best-on-fail driver still reaches a scored failure with the best written and surviving reload; the hint costs exactly one integrity; a combo's second word scores more than its first; an unknown word costs integrity and clears the slot; 16 corrupt-storage seeds across both keys survive with zero page errors; the idle menu does not spin the animation loop. Plus scoped `test:games`, `capture:games` (desktop and mobile both 0, screenshots reviewed at both sizes), `audit:contrast` (64 elements, 0 below threshold), all seven static gates, `npm test` 56/56, and `git diff --check` clean. ## 2026-08-12 Claude — overhaul campaign batch 2c: Flux Reversi gets a searching opponent, cascading flips, and flip previews

Third inline landing (see 2a for the strategy change that retired the parallel-subagent approach).

- **A real opponent.** The CPU was a one-ply greedy picker with a positional table. It is now a negamax search with alpha-beta pruning, move ordering by square value (corners first), and make/undo instead of board cloning so the tree is cheap enough to search deep. The evaluation weighs the positional table, explicit corner ownership, mobility, and a frontier-disc penalty, and slides toward raw disc count as the board fills — plus it **solves the endgame outright** once nine or fewer squares remain, so the finish is exact rather than heuristic. Casual searches one ply and a third of the time plays loose, though it still grabs a free corner. Standard searches three, Expert five. Measured: a bot playing the game's own expert-depth hint search **beat Casual 50-14, beat Standard 46-18, and lost to Expert 27-37**.
- **Hints** reuse that search from the player's side, always at expert depth, and ring the recommended square.
- **Flip previews.** Moving the marker rings every disc the square would capture and ghosts the disc you would place. Reversi is opaque to newcomers until they can see the consequence before committing, and this is the single biggest onboarding win in the game.
- **Cascading flips.** Captured discs now flip along each line with a per-disc delay scaled to distance from the placed square, so a big capture visibly ripples outward. Each flip is a squash through zero width with the colour swapping at the midpoint, over a rising pentatonic ladder so a long capture sounds like a run. Corners get their own fanfare and a board shake; placement kicks up particles.
- **Game over** now shows the final count, the margin, best margin, a win/games tally, and the level, instead of echoing the status line.
- **Mobile.** A 16:9 canvas squeezes a square 8x8 board into barely half its width, leaving tap targets under a fingertip. Below ~560 CSS pixels the canvas now switches to a taller backing store (960x900, with a matching `aspect-ratio` class) and drops the side readouts, roughly doubling the on-screen cell size. Desktop also had the board panel clipping the canvas subtitle and sitting off-centre; both are fixed. **None of these were caught by a gate** — capture and contrast both scored 0 throughout — they came from looking at the screenshots.
- Markup gained a hint button and three CPU-level buttons (plus a small `.barlab` label style); `flux-reversi.best.v1` and the sound key are unchanged so existing best margins carry over, with a new guarded `flux-reversi.stats.v1` holding level, hint preference, wins and games.

## 2026-08-12 Claude — overhaul campaign batch 2a: Last Light rebuilt as an eight-night lamp campaign (and a strategy change)

**Strategy change first, because it explains the small size of this landing.** Batch 2 was attempted three times with six parallel subagents and produced ZERO durable output: each attempt was killed by infrastructure (the 4am session-limit reset, a host-process restart, then an account usage limit), and in every case the agents died before their first file write. Subagent work is all-or-nothing against these kills. So this batch was done inline by the orchestrator instead, one game at a time, committing as each finishes — slower per game, but every completed game actually lands. Batch 3 onward should either keep the inline pattern or brief agents to write a working file to disk early and iterate, rather than composing an entire rebuild before the first write.

**Pre-existing broken WIP found in the tree** (predates the campaign; was already dirty at the 2026-08-11 session start, not produced by it): `fourfall.html` and `last-light.html` both carried uncommitted half-finished overhauls that were markup and CSS only, with no JavaScript. fourfall gained four dead buttons (`#hintBtn`, `#diffCasual/Standard/Expert`). last-light was outright broken: its HUD pill ids had been renamed to `#nightValue`/`#scoreValue`/`#chainValue` while the script still wrote to the removed `#movesValue`/`#resultValue`/`#solvesValue`, the `.touch` control block had been deleted, and an unwired `#nightPicker` and `#summaryDialog` had been added. last-light is resolved by this commit. **fourfall is still dirty with its four dead buttons and is NOT committed** — it is the next item.

**Last Light** is now an eight-night campaign instead of one fixed English-cross board. Every night is generated by REVERSE play — start from a single lamp on the target socket and undo jumps — so each board is solvable to exactly one lamp *by construction*, and the reversed build order is a guaranteed solution the hint system reads straight off. Six board masks (cross, diamond, courtyard, stairfall, wide, full) carry nights of 13 to 27 lamps. Night 1 rerolls its seed until the classic top-to-centre opening is still on the table, which both teaches the rule in one move and preserves the render harness's exact opening (Enter, Down, Down, Enter). Scoring adds chain bonuses for jumping the same lamp again (+15 per chain step), a 250-point finish, 500 for landing the last lamp on the gold target ring, a per-night bonus, and a penalty per stranded lamp. Stars (3 = perfect ring, 2 = one lamp, 1 = within three) gate the night picker and persist per night, alongside best score per night and a perfect counter. Off-path hints run a bounded depth-first search that asks for a *perfect* finish first and only then settles for any one-lamp finish, so a hint never quietly costs the gold ring; the 60k-node budget is reported honestly rather than guessed around. Juice: arcing jump tweens, extinguish particles, floating score, chain-scaled screen shake, a native summary dialog with badges and Escape/focus-restore, and a richer pentatonic synth whose pitch climbs with the chain. Mobile drops the in-canvas side readouts (they only repeated the HTML scoreboard and rendered as specks at phone scale) and gives the whole surface to the board.

- Verified: a 24-assertion Playwright probe — the exact capture-recipe key sequence still jumps; all eight nights solved to one lamp via the built-in hint path with 3 stars each and 13-27 starting lamps; scoring, per-night best, stars, unlocks and the perfect counter persist; Escape closes the summary and restores focus; 16 corrupt-storage seeds across both keys survive with zero page errors; the animation loop stays asleep when idle. Plus scoped `test:games`, `capture:games` (desktop and mobile both score 0, screenshots reviewed at both sizes), `audit:contrast` clean, all seven static gates, `npm test` 56/56, and `git diff --check` clean. Protected meta/JSON-LD blocks verified byte-identical to HEAD; no orphaned element ids and no dead controls remain.

## 2026-07-02 Claude — evaluated and REJECTED the build-time game-templating refactor (data-grounded)

The review's top remaining Tier-2 item was a build-time shared-fragment/templating system for the 100 self-contained game pages (a sub-agent estimated "~1 MB / 35% reduction"). Rather than write a speculative design doc, measured the actual shareability of the candidate blocks first — and the data killed it (turning the review's skepticism on the review's own recommendation):

- **Ambient backdrop:** only 46/100 games have the two-blob radial pattern, and it's 30+ near-unique variants — the most common exact string is shared by just **12** games. No shared block; normalizing would change dozens of games' looks.
- **`.pill` HUD rule:** long tail of per-game tweaks (top variant 13 games, then 6/5/5/4/4…). Not identical.
- **`:root` design tokens:** only 37 games have one, and **every palette is unique** (each game's own color identity).
- **`render_game_to_text()`:** 141 refs but every body returns game-specific state — not extractable.
- **Already-shared surfaces (correctly extracted):** the per-game meta+JSON-LD block (~2.5 KB/game, ~256 KB catalog-wide) is generated in place by `inject-game-meta.mjs` via `<!-- workshop-meta -->` markers; the storage bridge + `prefers-reduced-motion` reset live once in `workshop-runtime.js` (loaded by all 100).

Conclusion: the "duplication" is overwhelmingly intentional per-game variation, not extractable shared content. A templating system would homogenize the catalog's intentional visual identity (a `capture:games:ci` regression), save little, and add a build+injection subsystem + a drift gate + per-game migration risk for near-zero ROI. The sub-agent's estimate counted superficial similarity as duplication. Recorded the decision durably in `ARCHITECTURE.md` (new "Why game pages stay hand-authored, not templated" section) so it is not re-litigated; `test:architecture-doc` + `test:docs` green. This effectively closes the review's biggest remaining backlog item — with evidence, as a "don't build it."

## 2026-07-02 Claude — Brick Breaker: pause background music when the tab is hidden

Adversarial follow-up survey after the perf vein: checked two accessibility/polish veins and only one yielded a real, bounded fix.

- **Reduced-motion "gap" is illusory.** The raw stat (41/100 games gate canvas motion) suggested ~59 missing, but surveying the 16 candidates that pair a motion signal (shake/strobe/flash) with no reduced-motion read showed none are real gaps: wordle's shake/pulse/flip are CSS keyframes already collapsed by the universal `workshop-runtime.js` reduced-motion reset; echo-mimic/cipher-cadence/tempo-forge "flash" is essential gameplay (Simon pads, rhythm lanes) that must NOT be gated; the rest (blackjack, chromalock, clause-courier, eclipse-grid, floodgate, pylon-shift, seedline, market-minute, typeforge-cipher) are brief single-element `state.flash` feedback fades (~240ms on one card/tile), not vestibular-triggering. The genuinely motion-heavy action games already gate it. No sweep warranted.
- **Audio-on-hide: one real gap.** Of 11 continuous-music games, 9 already suspend audio on tab-hide. `rhythm-circuit` was a false positive (its "nextNote" match is a diagnostic field; audio is rAF-loop-driven and pauses when the loop throttles — nothing to fix). **`brick-breaker` was the one real gap**: a `setInterval` music sequencer (`musicTimer`) with no `visibilitychange` handler, so its music kept playing after you switched tabs. Added a handler mirroring the established flappy-bird idiom: `document.hidden → MUSIC.stop()`, else `musicOn && soundEnabled → MUSIC.start()` (MUSIC.start self-guards on musicOn/soundEnabled/audioUnlocked, so resume is safe). SFX are one-shot, so only the continuous music needed pausing.
- **Byte budget:** brick-breaker is a named-exception game sitting right at its deliberate 10 KB launch-margin floor, so the ~180 B handler tripped `test:page-weight`. Offset it by condensing three genuinely-verbose multi-line comments (the reduced-motion gate notes + the start-dialog keydown note) — meaning-preserving, established practice for this file — netting back to 114.9 KB / 10.1 KB headroom.
- Verified: scoped `test:games --slug brick-breaker` pass; scoped `capture:games --slug brick-breaker` score 0 desktop + mobile; `npm test` 56/56 (incl. `test:page-weight` back over the floor); `git diff --check` clean. JS + comment-only; brick-breaker is not in the PWA shell, so no `sw.js` bump. Handler logic verified by inspection against the proven flappy-bird pattern (headless audio/closure state isn't observable).

## 2026-07-02 Claude — Neon Snake: cache the per-frame backdrop gradient (perf polish)

Bounded performance polish from the review's Tier-2 perf finding (per-frame gradient rebuilds in hot draw loops). `snake.html` rebuilt its full-canvas backdrop gradient every frame (`createLinearGradient(0,0,w,h)` + two color stops at ~60fps). It depends only on the canvas size, so it's now memoized and rebuilt only when the dimensions change; output is pixel-identical. Left the per-segment gradient (position-dependent per snake cell) and the food-glow radial (position-dependent) alone.

- Implementation gotcha caught in verification: first attempt declared the memo as a module-level `let _bgGrad`, which threw `Cannot access '_bgGrad' before initialization` — an initial setup `draw()` runs before that `let` line executes, so the reference hit the temporal dead zone and killed the whole inline script (render_game_to_text/advanceTime never attached; capture score 240). Fixed by storing the memo on the hoisted `backdropGradient` function itself (`backdropGradient.grad/.w/.h`), which is safe to read before any `let`/`const` in the scope initializes. The scoped smoke gave the exact TDZ message; scoped capture only reported a vague "page/network issue", so running both mattered.
- Verified: scoped `test:games --slug snake` pass; scoped `capture:games --slug snake` back to score 0 desktop + mobile (pixel-identical, and the two different viewport sizes confirm the size-keyed cache rebuilds on a dimension change); `npm test` 56/56 (incl. `test:page-weight` — net +a few lines, within budget); `git diff --check` clean. JS-only; snake is not in the PWA shell, so no `sw.js` bump.
