## 2026-07-02 Claude — adversarial review follow-through: game-page input-safety gate + Pages artifact trim

Ran an adversarial review of the whole project (security, tooling/process, game-code duplication, git health) and then implemented the two safe, fully-verifiable recommendations. The review's headline: the product (frozen 100-game catalog) is polished and genuinely well-secured; the disproportion is in the engineering-health layer (93.5 MB `.git` for a 6.3 MB tree from 220/441 commits re-committing `progress.md`; ~2,500 lines of speculative owned-domain/launch-evidence machinery with no CNAME configured; 40% of the 75 `test:*` gates are meta; three full 100-game Playwright sweeps on every push/PR of a frozen catalog).

Implemented this pass (both green, `npm test` = 56/56):

1. **Game-page input-safety gate (`test:game-input-safety`, new `scripts/check-game-input-safety.mjs`).** Locks in the security invariant surfaced by the review: a game page is only sandboxed inside the player iframe — on direct navigation to `websites/<slug>.html` (and the player's "Open in new tab") there is no sandbox and no opaque origin, and every game ships `script-src 'unsafe-inline'`, so game-page CSP gives zero XSS defense-in-depth. The one thing keeping this safe is that no game ingests untrusted input. The gate scans executable `<script>` bodies (JSON-LD data blocks and external `src` skipped) with comments and string/template literals blanked in a single correct tokenizer, and fails if any game reads `location.hash`/`location.search`/`document.referrer`/`window.name` or listens for `message`. Two-view scan: identifier patterns run against the fully-stripped code; the `addEventListener('message')` pattern runs against the comment-stripped-but-string-preserved view (blanking strings would erase its own `'message'` token — caught and fixed during a negative test). GREEN on all 100 games today; verified it catches both planted violation types while ignoring inert JSON-LD / comment / string decoys. The audited `workshop-runtime.js` shim is the one legitimate `location.hash` reader and is not a game page, so it is exempt. Wired in lockstep: package.json, README (command + description + "55 → 56 fast validators" count enforced by `test:docs`), `.github/workflows/validate-catalog.yml` `catalog-docs-a11y` job step, and a new "Game page security invariant" section in SECURITY.md + Defense-in-Depth bullet.

2. **Pages artifact trim (`scripts/build-pages-artifact.mjs` PUBLIC_PATHS).** Stopped shipping `scripts/` (1.2 MB of Node build tooling) and `docs/superpowers/` (188 KB of internal AI plans/specs) to visitors — neither has a user-facing purpose and it needlessly exposed the internal tooling/planning surface. Narrowed `'docs'` to the two genuinely public docs (`docs/game-contract.md`, `docs/performance-baseline.md`). Verified the built artifact: `scripts/` absent, `docs/` = 2 files, `websites/workshop-runtime.js` still shipped, `fs.cp` creates the nested `docs/` parent. `test:pages-artifact` green (326 files). No PWA shell change (index.html/sw.js untouched), so no `SHELL_REVISION` bump.

Verification: `npm test` 56/56 (incl. `test:docs`, `test:test-aggregator`, `test:security-workflows`, `test:tools`, `test:pages-artifact`, `test:csp`); new gate positive (100 games) + negative (2 planted violations caught, 3 decoys ignored); `git diff --check` clean. No game/manifest change; catalog freeze untouched.

Staged, NOT done this turn (each needs work that can't be safely verified in one local turn, and the interlocking contracts — `check-docs-drift` hardcodes the 4-job workflow structure + exclusion list; `check-current-head-workflows` asserts 7 jobs remotely — are designed to reject exactly this kind of drift): (a) **git-bloat / stop versioning `progress.md`** — entangled with the user's instruction-file workflow (CLAUDE.md is required by `test:docs` to mention progress.md; `.ai-sync` already logs handoffs locally); a genuine workflow-preference call. (b) **park speculative owned-domain/launch-evidence gates** off the every-push fast run — touches run-fast-tests + the duplicated exclusion sets in docs-drift/test-aggregator + workflow steps + architecture-doc. (c) **scope render-capture/smoke to touched slugs on PRs** — needs a real CI run to verify. (d) **build-time partial/inline system for the 100 self-contained game files** (maintainability win; calibrated: injected meta block is ~2.5 KB/file ≈ 256 KB catalog-wide, not the 1.4 MB a sub-agent first claimed) and (e) **per-game playfeel/a11y sweep** (pause-on-blur in only 11/100 games; canvas-motion reduced-motion gating in 41/100) — both multi-session, frozen-catalog + byte-budget refactors.

## 2026-07-01 Claude — Checkers: hide the last-move highlight while a piece is selected (clutter + a fake "dim jump")

Follow-up to the move-pop fix. Two more highlight complaints while selecting a move: (1) the last-moved (black) piece stayed highlighted, and (2) a second, dimmer "jump" appeared next to the real one. Confirmed via the live DOM: black's last move `b6 -> a5` gave `a5` the `.last-to` (blue) + `.move-trail` markers and `b6` the `.last-from` (teal) marker. So (1) is the persistent `.last-to` blue marker on the moved piece, and (2) is the `.last-from` teal marker on the empty square the opponent moved *from* — not a jump at all; it just shares the teal/blue palette of the selection/jump highlights and so reads as a faint extra jump target while choosing a move.

- Fix: gate the last-move highlights (`last-from`, `last-to`, `move-trail`, `capture-hit`) on `!selected` in `render()` — the same rule as the move pop. They now show as brief post-move context (when nothing is selected) and disappear the moment a piece is selected, so the board is clean while planning and only the actual green jump/move for the selected piece shows. The "Last: X to Y" header text still names the last move.
- Verified in the harness: after black's AI move `b6 -> a5`, idle shows last-move highlights on `b6` + `a5`; selecting a red piece clears them to none (`lastMoveHi_whenSelected: []`). Scoped smoke pass; render-capture desktop + mobile score 0; `npm test` 55/55; no console errors. JS-only; checkers is not in the PWA shell.

## 2026-07-01 Claude — Checkers: stop the last-move "MOVE" pop re-flashing on every piece selection

Selecting a piece to consider a move re-flashed the "MOVE" (or "+N" / "KING") popup on the opponent's last-moved square, as if that piece had just moved again. Root cause: `render()` rebuilds the whole board on every interaction and re-creates the `.move-pop` element — restarting its `popRise` animation — whenever `feedback.activePopCount` is truthy. `activePopCount` is set to 1 on a move and only aged back to 0 by `stepFeedback`, which is driven by `advanceTime` (tests); in normal play there's no real-time loop aging it (consistent with the idle-rAF cleanup), so it stays 1 and the pop re-animates on **every** render, including the selection renders.

- Fix: gate the pop on `!selected` — render it only while no piece is selected. After a move, `selected` is null (`makePlayerMove`/`aiMove` clear it before the post-move `render()` at endTurn), so the pop plays as post-move feedback; the moment the player selects a piece to consider (`selected` set -> `render()`), the pop is hidden. No real-time timer or idle rAF added.
- Rejected an earlier one-shot approach (a `poppedMove` object-identity flag consumed inside `render()`): a single move triggers **multiple synchronous renders** (`animateApplyMove` + `endTurn`), so the flag was consumed before the pop was ever painted, hiding it entirely (harness showed 0 pops after a move).
- Verified: the async animated move path confirmed by reading `makePlayerMove`/`aiMove` (`selected=null` before the post-move render; `selected` set on the selection render). The synthetic-click harness couldn't observe the post-move state because `makePlayerMove` is async and locks the board during the animation. Scoped smoke pass; render-capture desktop + mobile score 0; `npm test` 55/55; no console errors. JS-logic only; checkers is not in the PWA shell.

## 2026-07-01 Claude — Checkers: add board coordinate labels (a-h / 8-1) matching the move notation

The move log shows algebraic notation (e.g. "Last: b6 to a5") but the board had no file/rank labels, so a player couldn't map the notation to a square. Added coordinate labels matching `squareName(r,c) = 'abcdefgh'[c] + (8 - r)`: files **a-h** left-to-right along the bottom, ranks **8 (top) -> 1 (bottom)** down the left.

- Implementation: external gutters — a `.board-frame` grid wraps the board with a left `.rank-gutter` and bottom `.file-gutter`. The gutters replicate the board's `repeat(8, var(--tile))` + `var(--gap)` + padding + 1px border so the labels line up exactly with the tiles (verified 0px offset on desktop and mobile). Labels are `aria-hidden` (squares already carry coordinate aria-labels); color `#9fb0c8` passes WCAG AA on the dark board.
- Density constraint: the render-capture gate caps **mobile** at 130 visible DOM elements, and 16 label spans pushed Checkers from 121 -> 139 (score 15 -> fails the CI gate). Since the cap is mobile-only and mobile had no labels before, the gutters are shown on desktop and `display:none` on mobile (drops them from the visible count, back to 121 — no mobile regression). Pseudo-element labels were rejected because `.sq` already uses `::before`/`::after` for focus/capture/jump states, and in-cell child labels would also trip the density cap.
- Verified: desktop labels aligned 0px ("87654321 / abcdefgh"); mobile 121 visible elements, gutters hidden, 0 horizontal overflow; scoped smoke pass; render-capture desktop + mobile score 0; `audit:contrast` WCAG AA pass; `npm test` 55/55; no console errors. Checkers page-weight has room (91.6 / 100 KB); not in the PWA shell.

## 2026-07-01 Claude — Checkers cover: pieces rendered as giant discs (symbol used without width/height)

The Checkers catalog thumbnail (`covers/checkers.svg`) drew its pieces as huge overlapping red/grey discs bleeding off the right edge. Root cause: the piece art was defined as `<symbol id="red-man" viewBox="-18 -18 36 36">` and placed with `<use href="#red-man" x=.. y=..>` carrying **no width/height**. Per SVG, a `<use>` of a `<symbol>` with no width/height defaults to 100% of the viewport, so each 36-unit piece scaled to fill the 640x360 cover — measured **300x300 each** — instead of sitting on its 38px board cell.

- Fix: define the pieces as `<g id="red-man">` / `<g id="black-man">` instead of `<symbol>`. A `<use x y>` of a `<g>` translates the content to (x,y) with no viewBox scaling, and the existing x/y values are exact cell centers, so pieces now render at their natural 30px (r=15 circle) diameter, centered on the correct dark squares.
- Verified: reloaded the SVG in the harness — all 22 pieces now render 30x30 at cell centers (57,19 / 133,19 / ...), zero oversized (was 300x300). `npm run test:cover-assets` passes (100 covers); `npm test` 55/55. The game `websites/checkers.html` uses `red-man`/`black-man` only as class-name strings, not SVG symbols, so it was unaffected; OG images are generated from manifest text (not the cover art), so no derived-surface regen was needed.

## 2026-07-01 Claude — Brick Breaker: bricks stretched + shoved down in the embed (resize scaled fixed-height bricks)

Follow-up the full-height player-embed fix exposed. With the iframe now growing from short to full height when the player opens, brick-breaker's `resize()` fired a large vertical rescale and stretched the brick field ~5x tall and pushed it into the lower half (huge bricks, empty top; ball/paddle fine). Root cause: bricks are laid out purely from **width** and fixed constants — `G.brickHeight` (18/22/26) and `y = G.brickTop + r*(brickHeight+padding)`, with `G.brickTop` keyed off `css.w` — so `css.h` never enters brick layout. But `resize()` multiplied `br.y` and `br.h` by the height ratio `sy`, corrupting the fixed geometry whenever height changed. Latent before (the old 150px iframe never grew, so `resize` never fired that jump); the full-height fix made it visible.

- Fix: in `resize()`, scale bricks by width only (`br.x`, `br.w` by `sx`); leave `br.y`/`br.h` alone. The paddle (re-anchored to `css.h-36`) and waiting balls (re-anchored to the paddle) already handled height correctly, so only the brick loop was wrong.
- Verified in the harness by forcing a 300 -> 1000px resize through `resize()`: the paddle re-anchored to y=964 (proving the handler ran) while bricks stayed top-anchored at y=79 / 147 (no y-scaling); before the fix they scaled ~3.3x down. Scoped smoke pass; render-capture desktop + mobile score 0; `npm run test:page-weight` holds Brick Breaker at 114.9 KB / 125 KB (10.1 KB headroom). JS-only; brick-breaker is not in the PWA shell, so no `sw.js` bump.

## 2026-07-01 Claude — Brick Breaker audit: fullscreen dedupe, click launch/release, drop aim arrow, cut render latency

Manual audit pass on Brick Breaker (game #1). Four fixes, all in `websites/brick-breaker.html`:

1. **Redundant fullscreen.** In the arcade embed there were two fullscreen controls: the player-bar toggle (a hard contract in `check-player-fullscreen.mjs`, deliberately fullscreens the whole modal to keep Share/Close reachable) and the game's own in-canvas button. Hide the in-game duplicate when embedded (`window.self !== window.top`, with a try/catch that treats a throwing cross-origin `top` as embedded). The standalone "open in new tab" page keeps its button.
2. **Launch/sticky release now accepts a click or trackpad tap.** The canvas `pointerdown` handler mirrors the Space key: release a stuck (sticky-power-up) ball, otherwise launch a waiting ball. Previously the stuck-release lived only in the Space `keydown`, so a click did nothing while the sticky power-up held the ball.
3. **Removed the misleading pre-launch aim arrow** — the small upward triangle above the resting ball. It always pointed straight up while the real launch angle is randomized (`randLaunch`), so it misrepresented the trajectory. Kept the non-directional dashed "ready" ring.
4. **Render latency under load.** `ctx.shadowBlur` (measured ~4.4x the cost of a plain fill: 0.32ms vs 0.073ms/frame for 300 additive circles) was set per-object in every loop that grows with on-screen activity — particles and rings (`drawFeedbackEffects`), explosion rings, bullets, and the bomb-brick glow. Because a slow frame is clamped to a 33ms sim step, dropping below ~30fps makes the ball move in slow motion, amplifying the felt slowdown. Dropped `shadowBlur` from those high-count loops (they already use additive `'lighter'` blending, so the neon glow survives) and kept it only on the low-count hero elements (ball, paddle, portals). Also cached the backdrop gradient + grid to an offscreen canvas, blitting it each frame instead of rebuilding a gradient + ~90 grid strokes every frame.

Verification: scoped smoke (brick-breaker) pass; scoped render-capture desktop + mobile score 0 (launch recipe + particle path intact); `npm test` 55/55 including `test:page-weight` after trimming added comments to hold Brick Breaker at 114.8 KB / 125 KB (10.2 KB headroom over the 10 KB named-exception floor); no console errors on load; standalone keeps its fullscreen button (`display:flex`) and launch-by-click confirmed (ball velocity set on canvas `pointerdown`). Brick Breaker is not in the PWA install shell, so no `sw.js` bump. A live in-browser FPS before/after was not capturable in the headless harness (rAF pauses when the tab is backgrounded); the perf fix rests on the shadowBlur benchmark + code analysis.

## 2026-07-01 Claude — player embed: the game iframe collapsed to a 150px strip (most of the screen hidden)

Manual audit of the in-page player surfaced a layout bug affecting **every game**: opened inside the arcade shell (the `#play=<slug>` modal), a game rendered into a ~150px strip at the top with the rest of the modal empty black — the paddle/ball and most of the playfield pushed below the fold. It only reproduced in the embedded player; **Open in new tab** (standalone `websites/<slug>.html`) and **Fullscreen** both looked correct, because they bypass the modal grid. Root cause: `.player-modal` is a CSS grid `grid-template-rows: auto auto 1fr` (bar | related | iframe) and `.player-frame` relied on auto-placement to land in the `1fr` row. But `.player-related` ("More like this") is `hidden` (`display:none`) by default, so it drops out of the grid and the iframe auto-places into the middle **`auto`** track instead of `1fr`. An `<iframe>` with `height:100%` in an indefinite `auto` track falls back to its intrinsic default height — **150px** — so the game only got a 150px viewport.

- Pinned the iframe to the flexible row: `.player-frame{grid-row:3;…}` in index.html, so a hidden related panel can no longer displace it. When "More" is shown, `.player-related` auto-places into row 2 and the iframe stays in row 3; when hidden, row 2 collapses to 0 and the iframe still owns the `1fr` track.
- Because index.html is the PWA install-shell root, bumped `sw.js` `SHELL_REVISION` + `VERSION` to the recomputed shell hash (`shell-a71270aebfdb`, `wa-v52`).

Verified in the preview harness at a 1900×950 window: the open player's iframe went from **150px → 891px** (= viewport 950 − player bar 59), `frameBottom` 950 with **0 viewport overflow**, computed `grid-row: 3`; the "More"-shown path fills correctly too (796px). `npm test` 55/55 (incl. `test:pwa`); `npm run test:pwa-runtime` green (worker control, cache-revision swap, offline replay); no console errors. CSS + SW-constant change only (no game/manifest change); CSP unaffected (`style-src` uses `'unsafe-inline'`, inline `<script>` hash unchanged).

## 2026-06-30 Claude — capture harness: stop Brick Breaker's flaky deploy-gate failure

The `Validate Catalog` deploy gate intermittently failed on `Brick Breaker desktop: 115 (interaction failed: did not reach brick feedback state; missing post-action render state)` and only passed on a re-run — a real reliability problem in the render-capture gate (false failures, manual re-runs), not a game bug. Root cause: the capture recipe launched the ball with a single `Space` press, but a keypress can be lost to focus/timing under CI load, and `brick-breaker`'s `advanceTime` only steps the ball once it is actually launched (`G.running && cardStart.hidden && …`, and `update()` skips a ball while `waitingLaunch`/`stuck`). So a missed launch parks the ball on the paddle and makes the recipe's 5000ms `advanceTime` feedback loop a silent no-op → "did not reach brick feedback state". Locally it never reproduced (0/30 faithful runs); a probe confirmed the mechanism deterministically (a parked ball yields `moving=false, reachedHit=false`).

- Fixed the recipe in `scripts/capture-games.mjs` (tooling, not a game): replaced the fire-once `pressAndAdvance("Space", 900)` with condition-based launch — re-press `Space` (up to 6 attempts, advancing between) until `render_game_to_text()` reports a ball actually moving (`!stuck && |vy|>0` or `|vx|>0`), then run the feedback loop. Robust to a lost/late keypress regardless of the CI-specific cause.

Verified: probe shows the parked-ball failure mode reproduces (the flake) and the launch-retry recovers it 20/20; `npm test` 55/55 (incl. `test:capture-recipes`); scoped Brick Breaker capture score 0; full `capture:games:ci` green (all 100 games). `git diff --check` clean. Single-file harness change; no game or manifest change.

## 2026-06-29 Claude — corrupt-storage robustness: 3 games white-screened on a stored "null"

Sweeping a fresh dimension — crash-resistance under corrupt localStorage — surfaced the highest-severity class of the session: **3 games (`circuit-draft`, `letter-foundry`, `relay-choir`) white-screen on load** when their save key holds the string `"null"`. Their storage helper guards malformed JSON with try/catch and a `value == null ? fallback : JSON.parse(value)` check, but `JSON.parse("null")` *succeeds* and returns JS `null` (no throw), so it slips past both guards; the value is then returned instead of the fallback, and `Number(saved.best)` / `saved.sound !== false` throws `Cannot read properties of null` during init — the game never renders. Reachable via a partial/corrupt write, a colliding sandbox key, or a tampered `#wa-storage=` bridge seed.

- Fixed all three identically: after `JSON.parse`, coalesce a `null`/`undefined` result to the fallback (`const parsed = JSON.parse(value); return parsed == null ? fallback : parsed;`). The other malformed values were already handled — broken JSON throws into the existing catch, and primitives/arrays return `undefined` on property access, which the `Number(...)||0` / `!== false` consumers tolerate; only `null` threw.
- New browser-backed gate `scripts/check-corrupt-storage.mjs` (`npm run test:corrupt-storage`) generalizes `best-score-guard` from "the best field stays finite" to "the game survives at all": for every game it instruments `localStorage.getItem` to record the keys read on load (no source-scraping), then seeds each with 8 malformed values (broken JSON + valid-JSON-of-wrong-shape) and reloads, asserting no uncaught page error and `render_game_to_text()` still parses. Registered as a slow/excluded browser gate (package.json, run-fast-tests `EXCLUDED_SCRIPTS`, check-test-aggregator + check-docs-drift allowlists, README, and the validate-catalog game-smoke job).

The gate is the verification: RED before (3 failures on `<slug>.v1="null"`), GREEN after (all 100 games initialize cleanly across 205 on-load reads x 8 malformed values). `npm test` 55/55 (gate correctly excluded, count unchanged); scoped smoke/capture/contrast clean for the three fixed games; `git diff --check` clean. Logic-only storage-helper fix; none of the three is in the PWA shell.

## 2026-06-29 Claude — blackjack: let the player bet their first hand (pre-deal betting state)

A 9-game scoring/economy sweep (negative/infinite balances, wrong-magnitude or double-counted awards, overflow, wrong payouts) came back **completely clean, high confidence** — idle-tycoon's geometric cost-scaling and finite-guarded earn, market-minute's affordability-gated cash, blackjack's payouts (even-money, 3:2 blackjack, push, double all correct, no double-pay), gemline's overlap-deduped match scoring, deckforge's clamped combat math, and dice-dynamo/circuit-draft/ledger-lanes/signal-siege all verified. No scoring defect anywhere. With the correctness dimensions (winnability, board-rules, economy) all swept clean, the best remaining concrete improvement was a real playability gap the earlier backlog flagged in **blackjack**: `loadGame()` auto-dealt the first hand at the default 10-chip bet, and `adjustStake` is gated off during `mode==="player"`, so a player could never choose their opening wager — and `newGame()` then said "Place your bet and deal" while a hand was already live (a false instruction).

- `loadGame()` now starts in a pre-deal `"betting"` state (empty table, no auto-deal) instead of dealing immediately; the hand is dealt only when the player presses Deal (`primary()` already routes non-`player` modes to `dealHand()`, and `adjustStake` already works whenever mode isn't `player`, so betting "just works" with no other plumbing). `newGame()`'s message is now honest ("Set your bet, then Deal"), and `draw()` shows a clear pre-deal prompt on the empty table. The verified-correct payout/deal logic is entirely untouched.

TDD red→green on the live page: raise the first-hand bet to 30 then Deal → RED before (mode stayed `player`, force-dealt at 10, bet unchangeable), GREEN after (lands in `betting`, no cards; bet→30; Deal commits the chosen 30, chips 100→70). The novel empty-betting-table initial render was the one risk — verified it keeps `capture:games:ci` at score 0 (desktop + mobile) catalog-wide, plus scoped smoke/contrast clean and `npm test` 55/55; `git diff --check` clean. JS/render only; blackjack not in the PWA shell. NOTE: this is UX polish, not a correctness defect — the scoring/economy sweep found none.

## 2026-06-29 Claude — checkers: the missing draw rules (it could never end)

An 8-game board-rules sweep (rule correctness, AI legality, terminal detection; chunked to dodge the rate limit) cleared 7 games with high confidence — fourfall detects wins in all four directions exactly at four and checks win before a full-board draw; flux-reversi enforces flip-required moves and a legal-only CPU; 2048 merges resolve once per slide with correct game-over; seedline/crown-circuit/tetris/solitaire all rule-correct — and surfaced one real defect: **checkers**, the same "game can never end" class as chess. `checkers` shipped with exactly one terminal — "the side to move has no legal move loses" — and **no draw detection at all** (the Game Over overlay even has a `'Draw'` branch no code path could reach). So a lone-king-vs-lone-king endgame (canonical, frequently reached) loops forever: neither side can force a capture, `generateMoves` always returns a king shuffle, and the game never concludes. Most visible in two-player mode.

- Added the two standard automatic draws, paralleling chess: **threefold repetition** (a `board + side-to-move` signature history) and a **no-progress clock** (`NO_PROGRESS_LIMIT = 80` half-moves with no capture and no man move — a non-capturing king move is the only non-progress move). New pure `boardSignature` + `drawByCounters` block and a `registerPlyAndCheckDraw` helper wired into both move-commit paths (`endTurn` and `aiMove`) after the existing win check; `showGameOver('Draw')` (already present but unreachable) now fires. Trackers reset in `newGame` (seeding the opening position), snapshot/restore through `pushHistory`/`undo`, and surface in `render_game_to_text()` (`drawType`, `noProgressPlies`, `maxRepetition`).
- New fast gate `scripts/check-checkers-draws.mjs` (`npm run test:checkers-draws`, now 55 validators) extracts the pure block and asserts the signature semantics, the threshold behavior (threefold at 3, the clock at its limit, repetition precedence), and that both `endTurn` and `aiMove` invoke the draw check. Registered in package.json, README, and the catalog-docs-a11y workflow job.

TDD: a browser probe drove a real lone-king-vs-lone-king shuffle on the live page — RED before the fix (gameOver stayed false forever), GREEN after (draws by threefold **exactly** on the 8th ply = the 3rd repetition, `winner=Draw`, `drawType=threefold`, `maxRepetition=3`; plies 1–7 correctly did not draw). `npm test` 55/55; full `test:games` (100), `capture:games:ci` (score 0), `audit:contrast:ci` (WCAG AA) all green; scoped checkers smoke/capture/contrast clean; `git diff --check` clean. JS-logic only; checkers not in the PWA shell. The other 7 board games were confirmed rule-correct — no further fixes from this sweep.

## 2026-06-29 Claude — shadow-vault: detection beats the exit (turn-order parity with shadow-switch)

A 12-game winnability sweep (chunked to dodge a rate limit; assessors verified generation by reading the code) cleared 11 games with high confidence — slipsort shuffles by legal moves, eclipse-grid/crate-circuit/prism-relay/packet-pilot/patchwork-foundry/moonbase-mutex/velvet-heist/glyphogram are authored with verified solutions, last-light is the canonical solvable cross, floodgate has no move budget — and surfaced one real defect: **shadow-vault**, the same win-condition-bypass class just fixed in shadow-switch. `tryMove` ran `collectKeys → checkExit → tickTurn`, so the floor was CREDITED the instant you stepped onto the exit, before that turn's spotting check (`tickTurn → resolveVision`) ran. You could clear a vault while standing in a guard's cone, the spot never counting (and, if it would have pushed alert to the 100 lockdown, it produced a contradictory "won" with "lost" flipped in behind it plus a stray stage-advance timer). Lower severity than shadow-switch — the alert meter only loses at 100, so the skipped spot is usually moot — but a genuine correctness defect and the best available move (the rest of the backlog is perf/cosmetic/refuted).

- Reordered to `collectKeys → tickTurn → checkExit` and gated `checkExit` on `mode === "playing"`, so the arrival-cell spotting (alert, and lockdown at 100) registers before the exit is credited; stepping onto a lit exit is now a spot, not a free clear, and a lockdown on the final step blocks the win (killing the contradictory won/lost edge).
- New fast gate `scripts/check-shadow-vault-solvable.mjs` (`npm run test:shadow-vault-solvable`, now 54 validators) mirrors the exact rules — fractional-speed ping-pong patrols, doors-aware vision cones, the alert meter — and proves all 4 vaults stay winnable under the corrected order (collect all keys, open all doors, reach the exit with alert below lockdown), plus that a reachable lit-exit arrival exists. Registered in package.json, README, and the catalog-docs-a11y workflow job.

Verification: the solver model went through three of my own bugs before converging (transient guard cycle, negative-edge Dijkstra → SPFA, OOM → parent-free reachability) — and it now agrees exactly with the independent assessor's model (two models concurring). A browser probe then cross-validated the model against the LIVE page: a model-found 21-move Level-1 stealth win matched the real game's player + every guard position at every step and completed (`mode=won`) on the fixed code, confirming the model is faithful and the fix preserves a real win. `npm test` 54/54; full `test:games` (100), `capture:games:ci` (score 0), `audit:contrast:ci` (WCAG AA) all green; scoped shadow-vault smoke/capture/contrast clean; `git diff --check` clean. JS-logic only; shadow-vault not in the PWA shell. The lit-exit bypass fix itself is verified structurally + by the gate's differential + the assessor; an expensive multi-level lit-exit browser replay was not worth it for low severity.

## 2026-06-29 Claude — shadow-switch: detection must beat the exit (no winning while standing in the light)

A parallel assessment of the remaining backlog (shadow-switch, doodle-jump, relay-choir, gridfront-orders, blackjack) found **shadow-switch** was the only genuine correctness defect; the others were refuted or low/cosmetic. In `shadow-switch`, `movePlayer()` credited the floor the instant you stepped onto the exit (`if (tryComplete()) { render(); return; }`) **before** `resolveTurn()` ran its per-turn pre-advance spotting check. So you could walk onto the exit while standing inside a guard's light cone and still clear the floor — defeating the game's core stealth promise (confirmed reachable on Level 1: when G1 sits at path index 2 facing up, its cone covers the exit cell).

- Threaded the completion check **into** the turn resolution: `resolveTurn(kind, sound, onSurvive)` now runs `markStep` → pre-advance `detectPlayer()` → `onSurvive()` (the completion hook) → `advanceGuards` → post-advance `detectPlayer()`. `movePlayer` passes `tryComplete` as `onSurvive`, so reaching the exit is credited only after surviving the same spotting check every other move already gets. `interactOrWait` calls `resolveTurn` with no hook, so wait/switch turns are unchanged. Stepping onto a lit exit is now a capture, not a win.
- New fast gate `scripts/check-shadow-switch-solvable.mjs` (`npm run test:shadow-switch-solvable`) mirrors the exact turn rules and BFS-checks every floor: all 4 stay winnable under the corrected order (the fix strands no level — the exit is always reachable on a beat it is unlit), and a reachable "step onto a lit exit" arrival exists (so the data exercises the bug) yet is a capture under the fix. Registered in `package.json`, README (command + description + `53 fast validators`), and the `catalog-docs-a11y` workflow job.

TDD: a browser probe replayed a model-found 18-step route to the lit exit against the **real** page — RED before the fix (`mode=level-complete`, "Floor clear"), GREEN after (`mode=game-over`, "Alert triggered"), with intermediate state cross-validated against the model at every step and a 17-step legit win still completing. Verified: `npm test` 53/53; full `npm run test:games` (100), `npm run capture:games:ci` (score 0), `npm run audit:contrast:ci` (WCAG AA) all green; scoped shadow-switch smoke/capture/contrast clean; `git diff --check` clean. JS-logic-only; shadow-switch is not in the PWA shell, so no `sw.js` bump. Refuted/deferred: doodle-jump (touch works; only an optional double-jump parity gap), gridfront-orders (no animation loop — idle-rAF defect does not exist), relay-choir (real but low idle-rAF in paused/solved states), blackjack (real but cosmetic first-hand-bet/status nit).

## 2026-06-29 Claude — chess: the three automatic draws (insufficient material, fifty-move, threefold)

`chess` ended a game on only **checkmate** and **stalemate** — `postMoveChecks()` had no other terminal cases. That left a genuine correctness hole that no render/smoke gate can see: a King-vs-King endgame (and any other dead position) could **never finish**, because no future move ever ends it, and a shuffling-but-drawn game dragged on forever. The half-move clock was already tracked (`st.halfmove`) but never read, and there was no position-repetition history at all.

- Added a self-contained, side-effect-free `>>> chess-draw-rules` block (reads only the plain state object): `positionSignature()` (board + side-to-move + castling + en-passant target, deliberately excluding the move clocks), `repetitionCount()`, `hasInsufficientMaterial()`, and `fiftyMoveDraw()`. Wired all three into `postMoveChecks()` after the mate/stalemate branch — insufficient material → fifty-move → threefold — via a small `declareDraw()` helper. Insufficient material matches the common engine set (K vs K, K+minor vs K, same-color KB vs KB; opposite-color bishops, two knights, and any pawn/rook/queen are *not* draws — mate stays possible). Threefold derives entirely from `stateHistory`, so it is undo-safe for free. Surfaced `drawType`, `halfmoveClock`, `repetitionCount`, and `insufficientMaterial` in `render_game_to_text()`; `loadFEN`/undo initialize/reset `drawType`.
- New fast gate `scripts/check-chess-draws.mjs` (`npm run test:chess-draws`) extracts that exact block, evaluates it in Node, and asserts the full insufficient-material truth table, the fifty-move boundary (100 not 99), the signature semantics, and the threefold count — plus that `postMoveChecks` actually calls all three (so the logic can never silently un-wire). Registered in `package.json`, README (command + description + `52 fast validators`), and the `catalog-docs-a11y` workflow job per the docs-drift contract.

TDD: wrote the gate first and watched it fail (`draw detection missing`), then implemented to green. A real-browser integration probe drove a genuine 8-ply knight shuffle through the live `applyMove`/`postMoveChecks` path and confirmed the repetition counter climbs 1,1,1,2,2,2,2,3 and the game ends precisely on the 3rd occurrence with `drawType:"threefold"`; bare-kings and a clock-at-100 FEN fired insufficient-material and fifty-move; zero console errors. Verified: `npm test` 52/52; full `npm run test:games` (100 games), `npm run capture:games:ci` (max score 0), and `npm run audit:contrast:ci` (all WCAG AA) all green; scoped chess smoke/capture/contrast clean; `git diff --check` clean. JS-logic-only change (no visual/DOM/CSS change), chess is not in the PWA shell so no `sw.js` bump.

Two low-severity screen-reader findings from the audits:

## 2026-06-29 Claude — neon-drift: stop the lap line auto-crediting after gate 7

The per-game correctness sweep found neon-drift's finish line (gate 0, at 214,420) sits only ~35px from gate 7 (180,410) — well inside the 64px gateRadius. Checkpoint detection credited the next gate purely on proximity, so the instant the car cleared gate 7 (checkpointIndex → 0), the very next frame auto-credited gate 0 and closed the lap with no driving of the final segment — a free checkpoint every lap, undercutting the "hit every gate in order" design.

Added a one-condition guard (reusing the existing `prevCheckpoint()` helper): a checkpoint only credits once the car has left the *just-cleared* checkpoint's radius. Every other consecutive gate pair is ≥129px apart, so when the car is within 64px of the next gate it is always >64px from the previous one — the guard is a no-op for all seven normal gates and only affects the 35px gate-7→lap-line transition.

Verified with a deterministic in-page autopilot (single `page.evaluate`, 1-frame `advanceTime` stepping, so the credit frame is sampled exactly): a full 3-lap race completes with all 8 gates credited (no regression), and on each lap the finish line credits only once the car is 68px from gate 7 — just outside the radius, exactly as designed. Scoped smoke green; `git diff --check` clean. One-file JS change.

The dimension a11y scout (and a re-survey of every text input in the catalog) found controls relying on placeholder-only labelling — a placeholder is not an accessible name (WCAG 4.1.2 / 3.3.2 Level A; it disappears on input and is not reliably exposed to assistive tech). Two gaps: the shared fact-match engine's primary guess box and answer-bank filter (4 games — cosmic-, hero-, night-shift-fact-match, arena-legend-guesser) and minesweeper's custom W/H/M number inputs (visible "W:"/"H:"/"M:" text only, no programmatic name).

- Extended `scripts/check-accessibility.mjs` with **Rule 5**: every text-entry `<input>` must carry an accessible name (`aria-label`/`aria-labelledby`/`title` or a `<label for>` association; non-text inputs are exempt). The shared engine is now scanned directly since it injects its inputs at runtime, so the class is gated catalog-wide. `test:a11y` runs in `validate-catalog.yml`, so a regression blocks the deploy.

Verified: `test:a11y` green (102 files scanned); proved non-vacuous (reverting one label flagged exactly `fact-match-engine.js:198`, exit non-zero); a live-DOM Playwright probe confirmed all 5 inputs expose their names ("Type your guess", "Filter answer bank", "Board width/height", "Mine count"); `npm test` 51/51; `git diff --check` clean.

## 2026-06-28 Claude — breachline keyboard guard (WCAG 2.1.1 keyboard)

The per-game correctness sweep found breachline's document-level `keydown` handler unconditionally `preventDefault`s Space/Enter and then runs `handleKey`, with no focused-control guard. So a keyboard user who Tabbed to any toolbar button (Execute, Switch Agent, Queue Wait, Next Mission, Restart, Sound, Fullscreen) and pressed Space/Enter had the button's native activation suppressed and a *game* action fired instead (Space → queue wait, Enter → execute beat) — corrupting the planned turn and failing WCAG 2.1.1.

Mirrored the corrective guard the sibling game bulwark-burst already ships: early-return from the handler when `event.target` is a `button/input/select/textarea`. The canvas is not a control, so on-canvas play is unaffected.

Verified with a custom Playwright probe: with `#soundBtn` focused, Enter then Space toggle sound (the button activates) while `beat` stays 0 (no hijack); with the canvas focused, Enter still drives the game (`beat` 0 → 1). `npm test` 51/51; scoped smoke incl. breachline green; `git diff --check` clean. One-file JS change, no CSS/markup impact.

## 2026-06-28 Claude — best-on-fail persistence across 9 more games (gate 5 → 14)

Two adversarial fan-out audits (six dimension scouts, then a full per-game correctness sweep of all 100 games) independently surfaced the same net-new class: **9 games displayed a live Best (`Math.max(best, score)`, or simply accrued score) but persisted `BEST_KEY` only on a win**, so a personal best set on the common *losing* run vanished on reload — a broken retention hook and a diagnostic-honesty bug (`render_game_to_text().best` reported a value that did not survive a reload).

Fixed the fail path in all 9 to commit best the same way each game's win branch already does:
- **bulb-brigade, signal-loom** — added the `write(BEST_KEY, …)` guard at the single `state.mode="failed"` transition.
- **moonbase-mutex** (`fail()`), **finale-foundry** (`fail()` → existing `commitBest()`), **beacon-bastion** (`fail()`, which uses `set`/`Math.max`) — one-line commit at the centralized fail chokepoint.
- **aster-vault** (×2), **comet-cartel** (×2), **canopy-courier** (×3) — commit at each inline fail transition.

Extended the CI gate `scripts/check-best-on-fail-persistence.mjs` from 5 → 14 games. Each new driver plays a real run to a *scored* game-over and asserts the failing score persisted and survives reload. Turn-based games drive with discrete key presses; real-time games advance deterministically through the game-contract `window.advanceTime(ms)` hook and steer from each game's own diagnostic oracle (`recommendation`/`nextNote`/`nextEvent`) — CI-stable rather than wall-clock-fragile (real-time entries flagged `realtime:true` for a 3-attempt budget; drift-loom folded into the same flag). The gate runs in `validate-catalog.yml`, so a regression now blocks the deploy.

Verified: extended gate green for all 14 games (run twice, stable; the adversarial verifiers confirmed each pre-fix fail path persisted nothing); `validate-catalog` (100); `npm test` 51/51; full 100-game smoke; scoped smoke of the 10 touched games; `git diff --check` clean. Pure fail-path JS in 9 game files plus the gate script — no manifest/CSP/SHELL/cover/meta impact.

## 2026-06-27 Claude — fix failing Dependabot npm updater (add .npmrc)

Investigated the lone red run on the catalog (a Dependabot "npm_and_yarn … for playwright - Update" job, run 28293954650). It was NOT the playwright bump's tests — PR #10 (playwright 1.60.0→1.61.0) is `MERGEABLE`/`CLEAN` with all CI checks green (Game smoke, Render capture, Performance, CodeQL pass; Build/Deploy/Live-Pages "skipping" is the normal PR behavior). The failure was Dependabot's own refresh job aborting:

> Error during file fetching; aborting: Private npm registries require either a .npmrc file in your repository, or explicit `scope`/`replaces-base` configuration in dependabot.yml. Registry: npm.pkg.github.com

Dependabot auto-injects a GitHub Packages npm credential (`automatic-github-packages-auth`), but with no `.npmrc`/scope the npm updater can't place it and aborts — so every npm Dependabot PR refresh was failing. The project uses only public packages.

Fix: added a root **`.npmrc`** pinning the default registry to public npm (`registry=https://registry.npmjs.org/`). This satisfies Dependabot's stated first remedy ("a .npmrc file in your repository"), needs no token (the `registries:`+`replaces-base` alternative would require one), and is a functional no-op for installs (public npm is already the default). Verified: `npm config get registry` → public; `npm ci --dry-run` → up to date; `npm test` 51/51; `check-security-workflows` intact; `git diff --check` clean. Confirmed end-to-end by re-running the Dependabot refresh after the fix landed on main.

## 2026-06-26 Claude contrast follow-up — chess board coordinates (clear a11y fix)

Resolved the largest cluster from the contrast audit (32 of 38 remaining findings). The chess board's a–h / 1–8 coordinate labels were `rgba(255,255,255,.6)` painted on the tiles, reading 1.18:1 on the cream light squares (`#e6e1ce`) and 2.34:1 on the blue dark squares (`#628aa3`).
